## Pass 352 · 2026-10-21 — all four prescribed regional legs ran and **all four returned material this base already carries**. That is the finding, it has a denominator, and the most useful thing in it is an **independent corroboration** of the deadline reversal `p351` published six hours ago — reproduced on a fresh leg that had not seen it

**Evidence:** [`regional-legs`](../evidence/regional-legs.2026-10-21-p352.tsv) · [`channel-bindings`](../evidence/channel-bindings.2026-10-21-p352.tsv) · [`star-cell-rederivation`](../evidence/star-cell-rederivation.2026-10-21-p352.tsv)

🟢 **Channel note, because it bounds every claim below.** `make hooks` **30/30 on arrival**. 🔴 **Every EU primary legal host — `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu`, `artificialintelligenceact.eu` — returns `000`, and the proxy names the cause: `connect_rejected`, a standing egress policy.** `p351` established this; it is **re-confirmed and must not be re-probed**. 🔴 **So every statute date below is SECONDARY-SOURCED by construction.** 🟡 Market sizing remains **unusable as a single figure** and is published as a spread, per `p350`.

### 🔴 The legs, and the number that matters most about them

| region | leg returned | anything NEW to this base? |
|---|---|---|
| North America | the US **state** legislative layer with a count, the federal **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026**, the **AFT National Academy for AI Instruction** | 🔴 **NO** |
| EMEA | **Digital Omnibus (EU) 2026/1744**, Art. 50 transparency, Art. 4 AI literacy, the **EC/OECD AI Literacy Framework (2026-06-18)** | 🔴 **NO** — but it **corroborates**, below |
| APAC | **Vietnam Law 134/2025/QH15**, **Korea's AI Basic Act**, the **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026** | 🔴 **NO** |
| LATAM | **UNESCO IESALC**'s 19-country study, the **UNESCO LAC Observatory**, **Colombia CONPES 4144**, **Uruguay** and the CoE Framework Convention | 🔴 **NO** |

🟢 **Checked rather than assumed.** Each leg's load-bearing token was put through this base's own `claims` discipline before anything was written: `134/2025/QH15`, `AI Basic Act`, `Digital Omnibus` and `1744` **each already appear on these shelves**. 🔵 **4 legs run, 4 returning relevant material, 0 returning anything new** — and that is a cleaner statement than either *"the legs returned nothing"* (false) or silence (which, as this base's own brief says, *"looks exactly like coverage"*).

#### 🟢 EMEA — the corroboration, and why an independent reproduction is worth more than a new fact

🔴 **`p350` sold an EMEA engagement against `2026-08-02`.** `p351` withdrew it: the high-risk education obligations moved, and *"the live one is six weeks out and it is a different article."*

🟢 **This pass's EMEA leg had not seen `p351` and returned the same correction, with both legs of the date:**

| duty | date | article |
|---|---|---|
| **AI literacy**, deployers — schools included | **in force since 2025-02-02** | **Art. 4** |
| **transparency / content marking**, new systems | **2026-08-02** | **Art. 50** |
| **transparency / content marking**, systems already on the market | 🔴 **2026-12-02 — six weeks out** | **Art. 50** |
| **high-risk** education systems — admissions, grading, proctoring, track assignment | 🟡 **deferred to 2027-12-02** | high-risk regime, deferred by **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** |

🟢 **So `p351`'s reversal is confirmed by a channel that did not inherit it, and the sixteen-month deferral now has its own date on this shelf rather than only a direction.** 🔵 **The engagement reading is unchanged and now firmer: the thing six weeks away is Article 50 content marking on systems already deployed, and the high-risk work a client will want to buy is a year further out than the Article 50 work they must ship.** `compose/patterns.md` carries the Article 50 recipe `p351` built; nothing in it changes.

🔴 **Declared gap, explicitly:** the EMEA leg returned **no school-level adoption statistics for any European country** and **no list of commercial EMEA players**. Two legs on two dates have now failed to produce either. That is a standing hole, not a quiet one.

#### 🟡 North America — a layer that is dense and a layer that is empty

🟢 The **state** layer is where the activity is: **134 AI-in-education bills across 31 states** in the 2026 session against **53 bills in 21 states** in 2025, privacy dominant (**California AB 1159** barring student data from model training; **Oklahoma** and **Maryland** requiring human oversight and barring AI from high-stakes decisions about students; **Georgia** and **Mississippi** adding AI content to computer-science credit). District policy is real and specific — **Katy ISD** blocks generative chatbots below grade 7; **Charleston County** bars AI as the sole basis for discipline or placement.

🔴 **Declared gap: Canada and Mexico returned no education-specific rule.** Canada has a national *"AI for All"* strategy (launched **2026-06-04**) with an AI-literacy push and **no statute and no legislative date**; Mexico has bills and **no regulator to enforce them**. 🟡 **For a North America engagement that is a two-country blank on a three-country region**, and it has now been blank on two consecutive measurement dates.

#### 🟡 APAC — the only region where a binding, education-specific, dated obligation exists

🟢 **Vietnam's Law No. 134/2025/QH15 is the sharpest instrument any region returned**, and this base already carries it: in force **2026-03-01**, extraterritorial, **education one of six sectors with identified high-risk systems** (automated assessment, behavioural monitoring), compliance **2027-03-01**, with a **six-month grace** for education, healthcare and finance systems already operating before **2026-08-15**. 🟢 **Korea's AI Basic Act** is in force from **2026-01-22**. 🔵 Singapore's agentic-AI framework and Australia's National AI Plan are **soft law**; China is the most prescriptive, with registration duties.

🟡 Sentiment, which a studio pitching APAC should know: the **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026** finds **lower** support for banning AI in schools across the Asian markets surveyed — and **higher** support in **Australia and New Zealand**. 🔴 So *"APAC"* splits on this question and a single regional pitch will be wrong in one half of it.

🔴 **Declared gap: no education-specific rule returned for China or Japan**, and the leg itself warned that *"policy data on South Asia and parts of Southeast Asia is thin."*

#### 🟢 LATAM — the adoption figure is strong, the governance figure is the opportunity, and both are higher-education only

🟢 **UNESCO IESALC, 200 institutions across 19 countries: 87 % use AI in at least one area of their activity, and 26 % have a formal AI strategy.** 🔵 **That 61-point gap is the LATAM opportunity stated as a single number** — adoption is already there and arrived bottom-up, *"driven by individual academics, researchers and students rather than institution-wide strategies."* 🟢 **The buyer is therefore governance, not adoption**: policy, data boundaries, an inventory of what is already running.

🟢 Institutions and instruments: the **UNESCO LAC Observatory on AI in Education**, launched **2026-04-14** at ECLAC in Santiago, with a Mexico pilot (**CONALEP**, **DGETI**) reported August 2026; **Tecnológico de Monterrey**'s UNESCO agreement (March 2026); **Colombia CONPES 4144**; **Peru**'s regulatory sandboxes; **Uruguay**, the first in the region to sign the CoE Framework Convention.

🔴 **Declared gap, and it is the same one as last time: everything here is HIGHER EDUCATION.** The leg returned **no K-12 regulation for any LATAM country** and left **Mexico's federal AI bill** unresolved. 🟡 Given that this base's LATAM supply rows are **K-12 and government-data** oriented — `Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil` carries **INEP** (ENEM microdata, national school census) and **FNDE** — **the demand evidence and the supply evidence are on different sides of the school/university line**, and that mismatch is the most useful thing to know before staffing a LATAM engagement.

### 🟡 No change to the canonical opportunity block, and that is a decision

🔵 This file's canonical **`## Opportunities by region`** block is **not edited this pass.** 🟢 **Four legs returned zero new facts, so there is nothing to add to it, and adding a re-worded version of what it already says would make the block look freshly measured when it is not** — the same reasoning by which `verticals/solutions.md` refuses to grow on a pass whose platform leg returned a listicle. 🟡 The regional *readings* above are new; the opportunities they imply are the ones already published.

## Pass 351 · 2026-10-21 — all four regional legs returned education-specific material, and the pass's sharpest number is a REVERSAL: `p350` sold EMEA against a deadline that had already moved sixteen months. The live EMEA deadline is **2026-12-02**, it is a different article, and it is six weeks out. APAC gained its first education-specific SUPPLY rows placed by commit stream, and the best of them is non-commercial

**Evidence:** [`channel-bindings`](../evidence/channel-bindings.2026-10-21-p351.tsv) · [`data-layer-licences`](../evidence/data-layer-licences.2026-10-21-p351.tsv) · [`identity-resolution`](../evidence/identity-resolution.2026-10-21-p351.tsv)

### 🔴 First the channel, because it bounds every regional claim below

🟢 **`make hooks` RAN** — 28/29 on arrival with one RED, **30/30** on exit. `p350`'s UNJUDGED state is resolved. 🟢 **The `git log` commit stream is OPEN**, so **four supply rows this pass are placed by instrument**, not inference, and each says which channel placed it. 🔴 **`api.github.com` and `github.com` are 403** — no star or adoption figure below is first-hand from GitHub. 🔴 **Every EU primary legal host returns `connect_rejected` at this session's egress proxy** — a standing policy, not a flaky server — so **every statute date below is SECONDARY-SOURCED and labelled as such.** 🟡 Market sizing remains **unusable as a single figure** and is published as a spread, per `p350`.

#### North America

🟢 **A federal bill moved, which is new on these shelves.** The **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026 (H.R. 8747)** was advanced by the House Education and Workforce Committee in a **July 2026 markup**. 🟡 Advanced in committee is not enacted, and no floor date surfaced.

🟢 **The state layer, consistent with `p350` and now with a count.** **134 bills across 31 states** in the 2026 session; privacy is the dominant theme — **California AB 1159** (barring student data from model training) and **Idaho SB 1227**; **Oklahoma and Maryland** require human oversight and bar AI from high-stakes decisions about students. 🔴 **A calibration `p350` did not carry:** in the 2025 session only **four** states (Illinois, Louisiana, Nevada, New Mexico) actually **enacted** AI-in-education laws. **Introduced ≫ enacted**, and the 134 is a bill count, not a law count.

🟢 **District-level prohibition is now the sharpest demand signal, and it cuts against student-facing products.** **Katy ISD** bans generative-AI chatbots for **K-6**; **New York City** has a **one-year moratorium on student-facing AI through eighth grade** — alongside the grading, discipline, placement and IEP prohibitions `p350` recorded. 🔵 **The engagement that survives this is the teacher-facing and administrative one**, which is also what the trend literature says works: *"the most successful deployments begin with teachers, not students."*

🟡 **Adoption:** **60 %** of US K-12 teachers used AI tools in the 2024-25 school year, **32 %** at least weekly. 🔴 **Canada and Mexico remain thin:** Canada's **"AI for All" strategy (June 2026)** carries an AI-literacy push but **no legislation date**; Mexico has bills and **no regulator**. Neither is a procurement signal yet, and that is written down rather than left as coverage.

#### EMEA

🔴 **`p350`'s EMEA opportunity rested on a deadline that had already moved, and it is withdrawn.** `p350` published *"the AI Act's high-risk obligations reach admissions and assessment from August 2026"* and sold *"90 % of the market is non-conformant against a live deadline."* **The Annex III education deadline is 2027-12-02, not 2026-08-02.** `p345` had this right; `p350` overwrote it. Full mechanism in `intel/trends.md` `T48`.

🟢 **The dating, on secondary convergence across five independent commentaries.** Regulation (EU) **2026/1744** (*Digital Omnibus on AI*) published in the **OJ 2026-07-24**, in force **2026-07-27**. **Annex III** standalone date → **2027-12-02**, education uses included (admissions screening, evaluation of learning outcomes, assignment of educational level, **exam monitoring**). **Annex I** products → **2028-08-02**.

🟢 **And the live deadline is six weeks out, on a different article.** **Article 50 transparency duties were NOT deferred**, and the grace period for marking content produced by systems **already on the market** ends **2026-12-02**. **Article 27**'s fundamental-rights impact assessment is **unchanged**. 🟡 Sources **disagree** on Article 4 (AI literacy) — relaxed scope versus already live — and that is published as disputed.

🟢 **So the EMEA pitch inverts in sequence, and gets NEARER rather than softer:** the **Article 50 transparency and content-marking kit against 2026-12-02** first, the **Annex III conformance programme against 2027-12-02** second. The **10 % of 450+ institutions with formal AI guidelines** figure survives as the gap — against the nearer deadline it is a sharper sell, not a weaker one.

🟡 **Sizing and leaders, flagged.** European AI-in-education **$2.64 B (2026) → $8.0 B (2030), 31.9 % CAGR** — **single market-research source, published as a claim, not a figure this base stands behind.** **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands** lead K-12 AI integration; the **UK** remains principles-based and sector-led via existing regulators; **Germany** carries a €20 bn AI commitment (2025–2030), not education-specific.

🟢 **EMEA supply gained a placement rather than a row.** `withmarbleapp/os-taxonomy` — already shelved — is now **placed EMEA by commit stream** (49 of 54 commits at **+01:00**, `withpebble.com` among the author domains). 🔴 **Its holder line names `Generative Spark, Inc.` with no country**, so authorship is EMEA and **holder jurisdiction is UNRESOLVED**; the channels are published disagreeing.

#### APAC

🟢 **APAC's supply hole is CLOSED this pass — two education-specific rows, both placed by commit stream.** This is the region this base has called its thinnest for four passes running, and the placement is by instrument, not inference.

- [`haolpku/K12-KGraph`](https://github.com/haolpku/K12-KGraph) — **11 commits from `stu.pku.edu.cn`** (Peking University), **5 from `hust.edu.cn`**, **15 of 16 at +08:00**. **NeurIPS 2026 Evaluations & Datasets Track**, accepted September 2026. 🔴 **And its dataset is `CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0` — NON-COMMERCIAL, read payload-first.** APAC's best education data arrives **unusable for a client build**, which is a sharper finding than *"APAC is empty"*: the supply exists, the licence excludes it.
- [`aclascollege/polymath`](https://github.com/aclascollege/polymath) — **12 of 14 commits from `aclas.college`, 14 of 14 at +08:00**; **ODbL-1.0 + CC-BY-SA-4.0, commercially usable.** 🔴 But it is **94 courses**, and its own `PROVENANCE.md` declares the course, degree, career and **salary-band** content *"synthesized"*. Usable as a **qualification-framework crosswalk** (ISCED-F 2013, CIP 2020, EQF, AQF), **not** as a course catalogue.

🔴 **Regulation: still NOTHING education-specific, for the FOURTH consecutive pass, and the leg said so itself** — *"the results contain no education-specific AI regulation."* The statute layer is general-purpose: **Korea's AI Framework Act in force 2026-01-22** with a ~1-year penalty grace; **Vietnam's Law on AI in force 2026-03-01**, risk-based, the first standalone statute in South-East Asia; 🟢 **NEW — Taiwan's AI Basic Act passed December 2025**; **China** binding across algorithms, deep synthesis and generative AI with penalties to **¥50 M**; **Japan** principles-based without penalties; **Singapore** guidance-only.

🔴 **And the market figures remain single-sourced.** Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson and Byju's named as APAC vendors with China, India and Japan dominant — **one commercial market-research report, directional only.** No national education-ministry AI policy surfaced, again.

#### LATAM

🟢 **LATAM returned usable denominators for the THIRD pass running, and this pass adds the breakdown that makes them actionable.** UNESCO IESALC, with UNU-IAS: **200+ institutions across 19 countries — 87 % using AI in at least one area, 74 % in teaching and learning, only 26 % with a formal AI strategy.**

🟢 **The new cut, and it names the buyer.** Adoption by institution type: **private non-profit 84 % · public 68 % · private for-profit 52 %.** 🔵 **The public sector is both the largest institution count in the region and the laggard** — so the governance gap and the volume sit in the same segment, which is the clearest engagement shape LATAM has produced on these shelves.

🟢 **Corroboration and instruments.** The **Digital Education Council LATAM survey 2026** — **30 000+ respondents across 29 institutions** — is a second, independent denominator. **UNESCO's Observatory on AI in Education for LAC** launched **2026-04-14** in Santiago. The **Latin American AI Index (ILIA), 3rd edition**, published **March 2026**, finds school-level AI education *"growing but limited to a few exceptions."* Teacher use: **Uruguay 75 %** of public-school teachers (Ceibal); **Brazil 56 % · Chile 55 % · Colombia 53 % · Costa Rica 52 %** against an **OECD average of 36 %** (TALIS 2024).

🔴 **LATAM supply is still zero rows this pass, and that is stated rather than implied.** No LATAM-placed repository was found on any leg. `p348`'s reversal stands — **LATAM will not grow a platform tier** — and `p350`'s measured **66 : 1** CPF/CNPJ-versus-CURP/RUT/DNI recogniser asymmetry remains the region's sharpest buildable gap. 🟡 It was **not re-measured** this pass; the figure is **carried from `p350`**, not re-derived.

The five regional subsections of `## Opportunities by region` below are amended as follows; the block's structure is unchanged.

- **North America** — add: **teacher-facing and administrative** scope wins where student-facing is prohibited (Katy ISD K-6, NYC through grade 8). Lead with the **60 % / 32 %** teacher-use figures, and quote bill counts as **introduced, not enacted** (134 introduced in 2026; **4 enacted in all of 2025**).
- **EMEA** — 🔴 **replace `p350`'s "live deadline" framing.** Sell the **Article 50 transparency and content-marking kit against 2026-12-02** first; the **Annex III conformance programme against 2027-12-02** second. The **10 % with formal guidelines** remains the gap.
- **APAC** — 🟢 **supply hole closed, with a licence caveat that is the whole brief:** `polymath` is usable (ODbL + CC-BY-SA) but is a **crosswalk, not a catalogue**; `K12-KGraph` is the better dataset and is **NonCommercial**. Regulation remains **general-purpose only**, fourth pass.

## Pass 350 · 2026-10-21 — all four regional legs returned education-specific material and LATAM returned DENOMINATORS for the second pass running: 87 % adoption against 26 % with a strategy, across 200 institutions in 19 countries. The supply-side news is that the LATAM gap this base has been pointing at is now MEASURED at 66 : 1

**Evidence:** [`latam-recogniser-supply`](../evidence/p350-latam-recogniser-supply.2026-10-21-p350.tsv) · [`channel-bindings`](../evidence/p350-channel-bindings.2026-10-21-p350.tsv) · [`unanswered-classification`](../evidence/p350-unanswered-classification.2026-10-21-p350.tsv) · [`alias-register`](../evidence/alias-register.2026-10-21-p350.tsv)

### 🔴 First the channel, because it bounds every regional claim below

🔴 **`make hooks` was REFUSED** (*"Code from External"*) — gate state **UNJUDGED**, 4th instance. 🔴 **The `git log` channel is shut** (credentials required), so **no demand or supply row below is placed by commit stream**; where a region is named, the channel that named it is stated. 🟢 The **raw payload** and **search-index** channels answer, and carried the supply leg. 🟡 Policy legs ran through web search only — no primary legal text was fetched this pass, so every statute below is **secondary-sourced** and labelled as such.

### 🟢 The four prescribed regional demand legs, leg by leg, with what each actually returned

| leg | returned | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **North America** | state-level legislative detail, bill numbers, district policy deadlines | 🟢 **richest policy leg**, as in p347–p349 |
| **EMEA** | AI Act enforcement dating, Commission guidance, one market figure, one adoption figure | 🟢 **answered** — and the dating is internally contradictory across sources |
| **APAC** | statute-layer regulation across 6 jurisdictions; **no** education-specific adoption figure | 🟡 **half-answered**, the same shape as p348–p349 |
| **LATAM** | a 200-institution, 19-country study with denominators, plus teacher-level comparatives | 🟢 **answered with numbers, for the second consecutive pass** |

### 🟡 The regulatory picture per region, as the legs returned it — secondary sources, labelled

🟢 **North America — the volume is in the states and the theme is human-decision and student-data.** 134 AI-in-education bills across 31 states on one 2026 tracker. **California AB 1159** would bar student data from training models; **Idaho SB 1227** requires privacy protections for school AI tools. **Oklahoma** and **Maryland** require human oversight and bar AI from high-stakes decisions about students; **Nevada AB 406** bars public schools from using AI for mental-health functions and requires a staff-use policy for AI in counselling. Four states — **Idaho, Maryland, Oklahoma, Virginia** — have 2026 laws requiring *both* state guidance and district-level policy; Maryland's 24 districts must adopt one, and **Ohio**'s district mandate fell due **2026-07-01**. **NYC** guidance uses a traffic-light frame and **prohibits AI for grading, discipline, placement and IEP development**. District practice is tightening too: **Katy ISD** bans generative chatbots for K-6 with supervised phase-in from grade 7 for 2026-27. On the build side, **Utah HB 273** puts AI into CS standards with supervised *"AI sandbox"* courses and **Virginia HB 1186** creates a pilot programme. Federally, the amended **COPPA Rule** reached its general compliance date **2026-04-22**.

🟡 **EMEA — the dates conflict, and the Commission's own statement is the one to quote.** The Commission states AI Act enforcement **from 2026-08-02**, with amendments adopted **June 2026** and in force **late July 2026**; secondary guides place high-risk obligations — education used for **admissions or assessment** is high-risk — at **August 2026**, while others still cite a February 2025 start. 🔴 **Publish the Commission's dating and the conflict, not a single number.** The Commission has also updated its **ethical AI and data in teaching and learning** guidelines, with AI literacy as a named concern. Nationally: **Germany** carries a **€20 bn** AI commitment for 2025–2030; the **UK** stays light-touch through existing regulators. One vendor figure puts the European AI-in-education market at **$2.64 bn in 2026** — low confidence. 🔴 **The adoption number is the one that sells a governance engagement:** across 450+ institutions, only **10 %** have established formal AI-use guidelines.

🟡 **APAC — read the statute layer, not the school layer, which is `p346`'s correction holding for a fifth pass.** **South Korea**'s **AI Basic Act** took effect **2026-01-22** with its enforcement decree, and MSIT has signalled 2026 as effectively a pilot year with a **one-year grace period on penalties**. **Vietnam**'s law took effect **2026-03-01** and its high-risk category reportedly covers education — 🔴 the source text was truncated mid-word, so **that is unverified and must not be published as settled**. **China** enforces binding rules on algorithms, deep synthesis and generative AI. **Singapore**'s agentic-AI framework is **guidance, not law**; **Japan**'s principles law carries **no penalties**. **ASEAN**'s Responsible AI Roadmap (2025) phases actions through 2030. 🔴 **No education-specific APAC adoption figure came back, for the third consecutive pass.** Written down rather than left looking like coverage.

🟢 **LATAM — the richest demand evidence on these shelves, and it has denominators.** **UNESCO IESALC** surveyed **200 higher-education institutions across 19 countries**: **87 %** use AI in at least one area and **74 %** in teaching and learning, while only **26 %** have a formal AI strategy — and adoption is driven by individual academics, researchers and students rather than institution-wide strategy, with public institutions more resource-constrained than private. Teacher-level, **TALIS 2024**: AI used in the previous year by **56 %** of secondary teachers in **Brazil**, **55 %** in **Chile**, **53 %** in **Colombia**, **52 %** in **Costa Rica** — against an **OECD average of 36 %**. **Uruguay** reports **75 %** of public-school teachers (Ceibal, 2026). Institutionally, **UNESCO launched the Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean on 2026-04-14 in Santiago**, and the **Digital Education Council** ran a LATAM survey with **30 000+ responses across 29 institutions**. **ILIA**'s third edition notes school-level AI education remains exceptional. 🔴 **No binding regional AI-in-education law was found** — the gap shapes the pitch rather than blocking it.

### 🔴 Market sizing remains unusable, and this pass can state the spread precisely

| source shape | 2025 | 2026 | terminal | implied rate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| IMARC | $6.4 bn | — | $79.6 bn (2034) | — |
| a second house | $8.3 bn | $11.4 bn | $57.2 bn (2033) | 25.9 % |
| TBRC | $7.52 bn | $10.6 bn | — | 40.9 % |

🔴 **Three houses, three 2025 bases that disagree by 30 %, and two rates that differ by 15 points.** 🟢 **Quote the spread — $6.4–8.3 bn in 2025, $10.6–11.4 bn in 2026 — and never a single figure.** Regional and channel splits are vendor-reported: North America largest share at **37.5 %** (2025, one report); cloud deployment **71.22 %** (2024). 🔵 Fifth consecutive pass in which sizing is recorded as unusable; the adoption/governance denominators above are the sellable numbers instead.

### 🟢 The supply-side news, and this pass can place it

🟢 **The component tier every region's education regulation implies now has a single MIT embodiment:** [`byerlikaya/Septum`](https://github.com/byerlikaya/Septum) ships PII recogniser packs for **16 jurisdictions** — `gdpr` `uk_gdpr` `kvkk` `lgpd` `ccpa` `cpra` `hipaa` `pipeda` `pipl` `appi` `dpdp` `pdpa_sg` `pdpa_th` `pdpl_sa` `popia` `nzpa` — behind a **human approval gate**. 🔵 It carries **no education token**, so the prescribed sweep could never return it; `p349`'s filter finding, now with a repository attached.

🟢 **England's statutory curriculum is available as a licensed knowledge graph:** [`oaknational/oak-curriculum-ontology`](https://github.com/oaknational/oak-curriculum-ontology), OWL/RDF/SKOS, aligned to the **National Curriculum for England (2014)**, under **OGL-v3.0** for the data and **MIT** for the code.

### Opportunities by region

#### North America

- 🟢 **Added p350 — the duty that four states now write into law is a PRODUCT, and `Septum` is most of it.** Oklahoma, Maryland, Nevada and NYC's guidance all turn on the same requirement: a **human decision between the model and the consequence**. `Septum`'s approval gate plus its `ccpa`/`cpra`/`hipaa`/`pipeda` packs is a buildable *"no AI decides alone"* control plane, and **California AB 1159**'s bar on student data reaching model training is a redaction-layer requirement the same repository answers. Pitch the control plane, not the tutor.
- 🟡 **Added p350 — the district-policy mandates are a services opportunity with a deadline attached.** Maryland's 24 districts, Ohio's 2026-07-01 mandate, and the four states requiring both state guidance and district policy mean hundreds of districts must produce defensible policy documents and show conformance. That is assessment-and-governance work, not platform work.

#### EMEA

- 🟢 **Added p350 — the 10 % figure is the engagement.** Across 450+ institutions only **10 %** have formal AI-use guidelines, while the AI Act's high-risk obligations reach **admissions and assessment** from August 2026 on the Commission's own dating. **90 % of the market is non-conformant against a live deadline.** `Septum`'s `gdpr`/`uk_gdpr` packs plus an Annex III evidence pack (`fairlearn`, synthetic cohorts) is the conformance kit.
- 🟢 **Added p350 — EMEA's education-specific supply hole is CLOSED, with a UK institutional row.** [`oaknational/oak-curriculum-ontology`](https://github.com/oaknational/oak-curriculum-ontology) is a national curriculum as linked data, permissive on both arms. 🔴 **Brief the split grant:** the ontology and data are **OGL-v3.0 with an attribution duty**, only the scripts are MIT — a studio shipping the data as MIT has mis-stated the licence on the part with the value.
- 🟡 **Added p350 — a 20-year MIT ERP lineage re-platformed onto Laravel 12 + Filament 5.** [`jtylek/EpesiCRM`](https://github.com/jtylek/EpesiCRM) is positioned by its own README as a kickstarter for custom ERP, and it scopes its vendored dependencies and an optional GPL-3 Roundcube explicitly. Placement is **heritage-and-holder, not a commit stream** — recorded as a signal.

#### APAC

- 🟢 **Added p350 — Korea's grace period is the window, and it closes.** The **AI Basic Act** has been in force since **2026-01-22** with penalties effectively deferred about a year. An institution that builds its conformance layer during the grace period pays for engineering; one that waits pays for engineering under enforcement. `Septum` ships `pipl`, `appi`, `dpdp`, `pdpa_sg`, `pdpa_th` and `nzpa` packs — six APAC jurisdictions already written.
- 🔴 **Added p350 — this region returned NO education-specific adoption figure for the third consecutive pass.** The queries were run globally and per region. What answers is enterprise AI-governance material and statute-layer regulation. **An informed gap:** APAC engagements cannot currently be sized from this shelf's demand evidence, only from its regulatory calendar.
- 🟡 **Added p350 — one APAC-placed Moodle integration exists and is NOT usable yet.** [`haanhtuandev/vgu-mcp`](https://github.com/haanhtuandev/vgu-mcp) (Vietnamese-German University, Go, Moodle over MCP) is **404 on all ten licence-ladder rungs** probed while its README answers 200. Ladder-absence, not enumerated absence — `ACTION ZZ-ao`.

#### LATAM

- 🟢 **Added p350 — the contribution opportunity this base has pointed at for two passes is now MEASURED, and it is 66 : 1.** Code search returns **66** CPF/CNPJ recognisers referencing presidio and **exactly 1** for CURP/RUT/DNI. All 66 are bespoke copies inside application code; **none is a reusable package.** Read first-hand, `Septum`'s `lgpd` pack defines only `TAX_ID` (CNPJ, **format-only, no checksum**), `NATIONAL_ID` (RG) and `EMAIL_ADDRESS` — **CPF is absent**, CNS and PIS absent. 🟢 **A checksum-validating CPF recogniser plus CURP, RUT and DNI, packaged against `data-privacy-stack/presidio` or contributed to `Septum`'s layout, is small, bounded and first-of-its-kind.** The 66 copies are the reference implementations; the packaging is the gap.
- 🟢 **Added p350 — the demand case now has a denominator pair that sells itself: 87 % against 26 %.** UNESCO IESALC, 200 institutions, 19 countries: **87 %** using AI, **74 %** in teaching and learning, **26 %** with a formal strategy, adoption driven by individuals rather than institutions. 🔵 **That is not a technology gap, it is a governance gap** — the cheapest high-value engagement in the region is institutional AI strategy and conformance, not another tutor.
- 🟢 **Added p350 — teacher-level demand is quantified and above OECD average across four countries.** TALIS 2024: Brazil **56 %**, Chile **55 %**, Colombia **53 %**, Costa Rica **52 %**, against an OECD average of **36 %**; Uruguay at **75 %** of public-school teachers (Ceibal 2026). Teacher-facing tooling has measured pull here, not assumed pull.
- 🟡 **Added p350 — a counterpart institution now exists to engage.** UNESCO's **Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** launched **2026-04-14 in Santiago** to help states shape policy, and the Digital Education Council has 30 000+ LATAM responses across 29 institutions. Still **no binding regional AI-in-education law** — the pitch is readiness ahead of regulation.
- 🟢 **Added p350 — a Brazilian academic row gained its canonical address.** [`IC-Redacoes-UTFPR/CorrecaoRedacao`](https://github.com/IC-Redacoes-UTFPR/CorrecaoRedacao) — ENEM essay scoring with LoRA fine-tuning and formative feedback — was published here under a personal-account address; the canonical is the **UTFPR** institutional one, `id` `1233297053`.

#### Global

- 🔴 **Added p350 — a correction to this base's own published count, re-derivable from its committed evidence.** p349's headline reads *"21 alias groups spanning 43 addresses"*; its own register file holds **22 groups and 45 addresses**. The `presidio` group was found late in that pass and written up as *"the 21st"* when 21 were already recorded. The **16 unknown groups** figure was correct.
- 🟢 **Added p350 — the identity instrument improved: the immutable repository `id` replaces HEAD-equality as the identity key.** It survives rename and transfer, cannot drift between reads, and cannot produce the same-HEAD false positive. Calibrated on `presidio`: `id` `132129752`, created 2018-05-04, now answers as `data-privacy-stack/presidio`.
- 🔵 **Added p350 — dead-address triage is a SUPPLY channel.** Classifying p349's 17 `UNANSWERED` addresses produced three platform rows (`opencast`, `EpesiCRM`, `gegok12`) and one EMEA institutional row (`oak-curriculum-ontology`). 🔴 It also found that **8 of the 17 were never addresses**: five are GitHub *navigation paths* (`topics/`, `orgs/`, `repos/`, `trending/`) harvested as `owner/repo`, two are planted negative controls, one is a README placeholder (`your-username/safetutors`). **The 2 462-address denominator every identity instrument here uses is inflated with non-addresses** — `ACTION ZZ-an`.
- 🟡 **Added p350 — `ECL-2.0` is a permissive licence with a NARROWER patent grant, and it is common in academic software.** `opencast/opencast` (509 ★) is ECL-2.0: Apache-2.0 with section 3 restricted so the patent licence reaches only claims where the author is also the inventor, bounded by the institution's funding-agreement authority. Immaterial for a lecture-capture deployment; a counsel question for a patent-sensitive product.

## Pass 349 · 2026-10-20 — the four regional legs were run again and LATAM returned the richest education-specific evidence of the four for the first time in this base's history; the supply-side news is that the component tier every region's regulation demands is EMEA-placed, and the sharpest LATAM opportunity on these shelves is a missing recogniser class in an MIT codebase

**Evidence:** [`compliance-vocabulary-grants`](../evidence/compliance-vocabulary-grants.2026-10-20-p349.tsv) · [`keyed-authors`](../evidence/keyed-authors.2026-10-20-p349.tsv) · [`education-token-leg-grants`](../evidence/education-token-leg-grants.2026-10-20-p349.tsv) · [`alias-register`](../evidence/alias-register.2026-10-20-p349.tsv) · [`alias-head-equality`](../evidence/alias-head-equality.2026-10-20-p349.tsv)

### 🔴 The four prescribed regional demand legs, leg by leg, with what each actually returned

| leg | what came back | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| North America | market-research geography pages; the governance gap this base already carries (10 % of institutions with formal AI guidelines, 71 % of US teachers untrained); a fragmented state-level regulatory picture with Colorado and Texas named | 🟡 **nothing new** — every substantive datum is already on this shelf from p343–p348 |
| EMEA | enterprise AI adoption and EU AI Act commentary; governance-first adoption in the UK, Germany, France, the Netherlands and the Nordics; the Middle East as a national-competitiveness track | 🔴 **no education layer at all.** Written down rather than left as apparent coverage: the leg named no ministry, no edtech vendor, no university and no school-level figure |
| APAC | enterprise tech priorities; Singapore's financial-sector AI consultations; Australian AI governance and copyright tightening; an enterprise survey (87 % encourage agent use, 47 % have governance); two corporate-learning deals | 🔴 **no education layer.** The only education items were vendor training alliances, not adoption or regulation |
| LATAM | 🟢 **the richest of the four** — a 2026 higher-education survey (92 % of students, 79 % of faculty engaging with AI; only 19 % of faculty using AI for assignment feedback against ~50 % of students supporting it), a UNESCO IESALC regional mapping of **200 institutions across 19 countries** (teaching and learning 73.5 %, research 57.0 %, administration 34.1 %, community engagement 20.0 %), ~30 % of universities with a published AI policy, an Inter-American Development Bank regulatory framework, and the CEPAL-linked ILIA index scoring 19 countries | 🟢 **substantive, and it inverts this base's standing expectation** |

🔵 **The inversion worth stating plainly.** For six passes this base recorded LATAM as the region it could not evidence. 🟢 **This pass it is the only region whose education-specific adoption, use-area breakdown and policy coverage all came back with denominators**, while EMEA and APAC returned enterprise governance. 🔴 **The caveat travels with it:** the survey covers **29 institutions**, the figures are self-reported, and both publishers have an interest in the finding — so it is a demand signal with a named population, not a market size.

### 🟡 The regulatory picture per region, as the legs returned it this pass

🔴 **LATAM has no unified framework and that is the engagement.** Chile has had a national AI policy since 2021 with a bill under discussion; Brazil and Colombia have national strategies with **no education-specific rules**; Mexico's official bodies have issued **non-binding** recommendations. 🟢 So a LATAM institution cannot buy "compliance" off a shelf — it buys a defensible position, which is a policy, a data boundary and an evidence pack.
🟡 **EMEA's instrument is the binding one and its education tier is deferred to 2027-12-02**, which this base has carried since p345 and the leg did not move.
🟡 **North America stays sub-federal**: a state patchwork rather than a rulebook.
🟡 **APAC is converging on principles and executing per market**, on an enterprise rather than education track.

### 🟢 The supply-side news, and it is placed

🔴 **Eleven permissive compliance components, absent from all nine of these shelves until this pass, and six of the eight that could be placed by commit stream are EMEA.** `presidio` (**MIT**, 894 × `+0200` of 1 645 commits), `arx` (**Apache-2.0**, 2 809 × `+0200` of 4 306), `diffprivlib` (**MIT**, 346 × `+0100` of 596), `greenmask` (**Apache-2.0**, 325 × `+0300` of 586), `databunker` (**MIT**, 541 × `+0200` of 1 160), `rizzo-pii` (**MIT**, 138 × `+0200` of 144). 🟢 **One North America** (`top_secret`, **MIT**, 55 × `−0400` of 60) and **one APAC** (`hitsz-ids/synthetic-data-generator`, **Apache-2.0**, 250 × `+0800` of 281). 🔵 **The region whose regulation is hardest is also where the components are built** — which is a staffing fact for a studio, not only a licence fact.

### Opportunities by region

#### North America

- 🟡 **Added p349 — a correction before an opportunity: the credentials row this file carries is one repository under two addresses.** [`openwallet-foundation-labs/learner-credential-wallet`](https://github.com/openwallet-foundation-labs/learner-credential-wallet) is the canonical address; `digitalcredentials/learner-credential-wallet` is **STALE** and resolves to the same pin `1c46a82ba384`. 🟢 The steward question p348 raised is settled: ask who maintains it *before* asking what it is licensed as.
- 🟢 **Added p349 — the one NA-placed compliance component, and it is the cheapest possible start.** [`thoughtbot/top_secret`](https://github.com/thoughtbot/top_secret) (**MIT**, `LICENSE.txt` 1 095 B, 60 commits, 7 author addresses (`%ae`), 55 × `−0400`) filters sensitive text before it leaves a Ruby stack for a model. 🔵 For a district or a state system already in the 56-point governance gap, a request-path filter is a two-week deliverable that changes the DPO conversation.

#### EMEA

- 🟢 **Added p349 — the component tier that the EU AI Act's education duties imply is BUILT HERE, and six rows prove it.** `presidio`, `arx`, `diffprivlib`, `greenmask`, `databunker` and `rizzo-pii` are all permissive and all EMEA by commit stream. 🔵 **Sell the evidence pack, not the tool**: ε from `opacus` or `diffprivlib`, a re-identification risk figure from `arx`, a disparity report from `fairlearn`, in one deliverable.
- 🔴 **Added p349 — this region returned NOTHING education-specific on the prescribed leg, for the second consecutive pass.** The queries run are recorded in the leg table above; every result was enterprise AI or general AI Act commentary. 🟢 An informed gap: an EMEA engagement must be scoped from the instrument (Annex III, deferred to 2027-12-02) rather than from adoption data this base does not have.

#### APAC

- 🟡 **Added p349 — the holder line is now a 2-for-2 APAC placement channel on one specific form.** `PT NAKAFA TEKNO KREATIF` and p348's `PT Motiolabs Digital Indonesia` are Indonesian statutory company forms read straight out of the grant. 🔴 One of them, [`nakafaai/nakafa.com`](https://github.com/nakafaai/nakafa.com), is **noncommercial and not OSI** (7 705 B) — **unusable under `P250`**, recorded so no later pass spends a probe.
- 🟢 **Added p349 — the APAC-placed compliance row.** [`hitsz-ids/synthetic-data-generator`](https://github.com/hitsz-ids/synthetic-data-generator) (**Apache-2.0**, 281 commits, 25 author addresses (`%ae`), 250 × `+0800`) generates structured tabular data — the substitute for a cohort export in a jurisdiction that will not permit one. 🔴 Dormant since **2025-03-06**: price patch-carrying into the quote.
- 🔴 **Added p349 — no education-specific adoption or regulation figure came back on the prescribed leg.** What answered was an enterprise governance gap (87 % encourage agent use, 47 % have governance) and two corporate-learning alliances. 🟢 Declared rather than silently absent.

#### LATAM

- 🟢 **Added p349 — the most concrete LATAM contribution opportunity this base has ever been able to name, and it is measured.** [`data-privacy-stack/presidio`](https://github.com/data-privacy-stack/presidio) (**MIT**) ships country-specific recogniser sets for **18 countries** — australia, canada, finland, germany, india, italy, korea, nigeria, philippines, poland, singapore, south_africa, spain, sweden, thai, turkey, uk, us — enumerated over the complete tree at the pin `4f0e236e31a5`. 🔴 **Not one Latin American country: no CPF, no CNPJ, no CURP, no RUT, no DNI.** 🟢 **The leading open PII engine cannot redact a Brazilian or Mexican student record**, and the fix is a recogniser class plus a test fixture in an MIT codebase — low cost, high visibility, and it makes the studio a named contributor to a layer every regional competitor will need.
- 🟢 **Added p349 — the region's demand evidence now has denominators.** 92 % of students and 79 % of faculty engaging with AI (29-institution survey); 73.5 % of institutions using AI in teaching and learning against 34.1 % in administration (UNESCO IESALC, 200 institutions, 19 countries); **~30 % of universities with a published AI policy.** 🔵 **The administration figure is the sellable one**: two thirds of institutions have not touched the back office, which is where an engagement has a measurable before and after.
- 🟡 **Added p349 — no unified regulatory framework, and that shapes the pitch rather than blocking it.** Chile has a national policy and a bill; Brazil and Colombia have strategies without education-specific rules; Mexico's recommendations are non-binding. 🟢 Sell a defensible position — policy, data boundary, evidence pack — not "compliance with" a statute that does not exist yet.
- 🟡 **Added p349 — this region's LATAM-placed supply row has a casing correction.** The canonical address is [`Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil`](https://github.com/Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil) as the search index returns it; `dasgltd/mcp-brasil` is **STALE** at the same pin `2efb258370b1`.

#### Global

- 🟢 **Added p349 — a client-facing artefact came out of this pass's identity work, and it is quotable.** The [`alias-register`](../evidence/alias-register.2026-10-20-p349.tsv) (46 rows: group, address, state, pin, deciding channel) is what makes an option count defensible. 🔴 **Before it, this base would have quoted 21 groups' worth of duplicates as distinct options** — including, in four live files, one credential wallet as two.
- 🔵 **Added p349 — the licence posture of the compliance tier is region-neutral, which is the reason it travels.** Fourteen of fifteen candidates are MIT, Apache-2.0 or BSD, so the same component answers a Brazilian data-protection question, an EU Annex III question and a US state-patchwork question without a second procurement.

## Pass 348 · 2026-10-20 — all four regional legs returned education-specific policy again, and the supply-side news is a REVERSAL of this base's standing expectation: LATAM will not grow a platform tier, and the compliance components every region's regulation demands carry no education token at all

**Evidence:** [`p348-policy-channel`](../evidence/p348-policy-channel.2026-10-20-p348.tsv) · [`p348-institutional-orphan-probe`](../evidence/p348-institutional-orphan-probe.2026-10-20-p348.tsv) · [`p348-placement-and-metadata`](../evidence/p348-placement-and-metadata.2026-10-20-p348.tsv)

### 🔴 First, the channel, because it bounds everything below — and this pass corrects how the limit is described

| host | `curl` | the second fetch channel | what it means for a successor |
|---|---|---|---|
| `github.com` HTML | 🔴 403 | 🟢 **200** | a **PER-TOOL** block. 🟢 **Retry it on another channel** — this is how every star count in this pass was read, after two passes recorded stars as unreadable |
| `api.github.com` | 🔴 403 | — | per-tool block |
| `eur-lex.europa.eu` · `education.ec.europa.eu` · `www.future-ed.org` · `unesco.org` · `oecd.org` | 🔴 000 | 🔴 **ENOTFOUND** | **NAME RESOLUTION.** 🔴 **No tool choice fixes it.** Do not retry |
| `raw.githubusercontent.com` · `www.apache.org` | 🟢 200/301 | — | why every licence cell here is first-hand |

🟢 **So the rule is sharper than p347's: a 403 is worth another channel, an ENOTFOUND is not.** 🔴 **Every regulatory date on these shelves therefore stays labelled search-channel convergence**, and where trackers disagree the SPREAD is published rather than a number.

### 🟢 The four prescribed regional legs, each split into the two verdicts `ACTION ZZ-k` requires

| region | SUPPLY verdict | POLICY verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **North America** | 🟢 **Strong and deepening.** Four placed rows re-read at the pin this pass, two of them pushed on the day of the pass: `fairlearn/fairlearn` (MIT, 2 300 ★, 139 author names), `terracotta-education/terracotta` (Apache-2.0, 2 815 c), `datakind/student-success-tool` (MIT, 3 015 c), `digitalcredentials/learner-credential-wallet` (MIT, 1 309 c) | 🟡 **A patchwork with a volume spread, not a rulebook.** 134 / 77 / 52 bills across 31 / 27 / 25 states depending on the tracker; California **AB 1159** barring student data from model training; Oklahoma **SB 1734** requiring human-in-the-loop and barring AI as the main basis for grading or discipline, with a parental opt-out; Alabama **HB 329** making an AI-inclusive CS course a graduation requirement; Maryland **SB 720** requiring district policies and AI coordinators; Ohio setting **July 1** for every district to adopt a formal AI policy. Opposite poles in the same session: New York **A.9190** would ban classroom AI below grade 9, Utah **SB 322** builds a red-team sandbox |
| **EMEA** | 🟢 **This base's deepest tier, and it gained its integrity and benchmarking layers this pass.** `dodona-edu/dolos` (MIT, Ghent, 3 859 c, three placement channels agreeing), `DFE-Digital/education-benchmarking-and-insights` (MIT, **5 266 c**, pushed today), `examplary/qti` (MIT, `+0100` on all 39 commits), `AI-for-Education/*` — a UK consultancy's cluster, of which `voice-ai-evaluation-framework` evaluates ASR for **early-grade reading assessment in Kiswahili child speech** | 🟢 **Regulation leads adoption, which is the region's whole opportunity.** **AILit** (Commission + OECD, presented 2026-06-17, four domains, nineteen competences, built with CodeAI from 2 000+ contributors, non-binding, feeding **PISA 2029**); Article 4's AI-literacy duty binding on every AI-using organisation — therefore every school — since **2025-02-02**; Annex III education high-risk at **2027-12-02**, deferred from 2026-08-02 by the Digital Omnibus; an EACEA squad mapping Member-State implementation **2026-10 → 2027-01**. 🔴 Sources **conflict** on whether the Omnibus altered Article 4 — one reads it as replaced by a duty to *"support the development of"* AI literacy, another as unchanged. **This base publishes neither reading** |
| **APAC** | 🟡 **One new bench, APAC-engineered, vendor-owned, and explicitly not production.** `open-edge-platform/education-ai-suite` (Apache-2.0, Smart Classroom + Teaching Assistant on OpenVINO, `+0800`/`+0530` = 82 % of the stream, pushed today, **README says reference and demonstration, NOT production**); `motiolabs-space/open-academic` (MIT, Indonesia, `+0700` on all 75 commits, 1 author) | 🟢 **Education is in statute here, not in guidance.** Korea's **AI Basic Act** in force **2026-01-22** — the region's first comprehensive mandatory AI law; Vietnam's effective **2026-03-01**; China with no general AI law but enforceable specifics (AI-content labelling; model filing covering **988 services** by 2026-06-30) and AI reported compulsory from age six; India with no AI statute, governing through data-protection rules phasing to **May 2027**, while making **AI and computational thinking compulsory from Class 3** in the 2026-27 academic year under the IndiaAI Mission; Japan's AI Promotion Act with no fines and no prohibitions. Ipsos Education Monitor 2026: Asian markets show **lower** support for banning AI in schools than for restricting children's social media, while **Australia and New Zealand show higher** support for a ban |
| **LATAM** | 🔴 **Two new placed rows, 6 commits and 3 commits, and a REVERSAL of this base's standing expectation.** `latam-gpt/anonymization-filter` (MIT, `−0400` on all 6, CENIA/Chile) and `Jeanikt/tutor-ai-agent` (MIT, `−0300` on all 3, Brazil, a LiveKit template derivative). The regional token sweep over the whole 528-row inventory returned **14 candidates**: two LATAM-authored, one a **Polish vendor**, one an **EleutherAI fork**, the rest UK or US. 🟢 **Stop waiting for a LATAM platform tier** | 🟢 **The strongest demand numbers of any region, all on the DEMAND side.** UNESCO: **nine in ten** 15-year-olds across the 13 regional PISA 2025 countries have already used AI chatbots for schoolwork. TALIS 2024: teacher AI use **56 % Brazil**, **55 % Chile**, against an **OECD average of 36 %**. Regulation still prospective: Brazil's **PL 2338/2023** in process with education **explicitly** in its high-risk category (mandatory impact assessments for schools and universities if enacted); Chile's bill pending and principles-based; Mexico's 2026 amendments covering deepfakes, performers' rights and enforcement — **not education** — with sources disagreeing whether they constitute a general AI law. Tec de Monterrey redesigning **44+** degree programmes to integrate AI |

### 🔴 The supply-side reversal, and it is a FILTER finding rather than a market finding

🟡 Three of four regions now have a binding instrument whose hardest duty is **not** about AI capability: student-data protection, demonstrated non-discrimination, and a human decision between the model and the consequence. 🔴 **Not one of the components that answer those duties carries an education token** — `fairlearn/fairlearn` for group-fairness evidence, the privacy and synthetic-data tier, audit logging. 🟢 **So every education-token filter this base has ever run is structurally blind to the compliance half of its own market**, which is why a 2 300-star MIT library that EMEA's Annex III duty effectively requires took until pass 348 to appear on these shelves. 🔵 **An industry KB that selects on industry vocabulary will systematically miss the HORIZONTAL components that industry's regulation demands.** `ACTION ZZ-ae` runs the compliance-vocabulary leg.

### 🟡 Market sizing remains unusable, and this pass can state the spread rather than the reason

🔴 AI-in-education 2026: **USD 9.58B / 10.6B / 11.4B** from three firms, 2030–2035 endpoints **42.48B** and **136.79B**. The narrower **AI-tutors** segment: **2.7B → 17.7B (2033)** from one firm, **6.6B → 80.4B (2034)** from another. 🔵 **An order of magnitude apart on the same object, from firms that each define it differently.** 🟢 `ACTION ZQ` holds: **attribute or omit**, and prefer the compound first-hand measurements this base can actually defend.

---

## Pass 347 · 2026-10-20 — all four regional legs returned education-specific policy, and the supply side was carried by the inventory for the third pass running: this is the first pass in which every region gains a placed row

**Evidence:** [`p347-policy-channel-reachability`](../evidence/p347-policy-channel-reachability.2026-10-20-p347.tsv) · [`p347-institutional-orphan-probe`](../evidence/p347-institutional-orphan-probe.2026-10-20-p347.tsv).

### 🔴 First, the channel, because it bounds everything below

🔵 p345 labelled its EU date resolution a **convergence of secondary sources** that may not be cited as a verified read, because every EU domain returned HTTP 000. 🟢 **This pass tested whether a second, independent fetch channel reaches any policy primary. It does not** ([`p347-policy-channel-reachability`](../evidence/p347-policy-channel-reachability.2026-10-20-p347.tsv)): `curl` fails at the proxy/TLS layer with **HTTP 000** and the WebFetch channel fails at **DNS (`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`)** on the same hosts — `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu`, `artificialintelligenceact.eu`, `iesalc.unesco.org`, `unesco.org`, `oecd.org`, `congress.gov`, `indicelatam.cl`. 🟡 **So the limitation is structural rather than a channel choice, and it will not be fixed by trying harder.** 🟢 Two hosts answer: `raw.githubusercontent.com` (which is why every licence cell on these shelves is a first-hand read) and `www.apache.org` (which is why an Apache-2.0 notice-by-reference is checkable here). 🔵 **Every date and percentage in this section is therefore SEARCH-CHANNEL CONVERGENCE, labelled as such**, and `gate-dates` remains the instrument that keeps a stale one off the shelves: **Annex III education duties `2027-12-02`; Article 50 transparency live since `2026-08-02`.**

### 🟢 The four prescribed regional legs, each split into the two verdicts `ACTION ZZ-k` requires

| Region | Policy / demand verdict | Supply verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **North America** | 🟢 **Not empty, and it is now sub-federal.** The search channel converges on **134 AI-in-education bills across 31 states** in the 2026 session, with named instances: **California AB 1159** barring the use of student data to train models, and Oklahoma and Maryland rules requiring human oversight and barring AI from high-stakes student decisions. Federally, the **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026 (H.R. 8747)** was reported out of the House Education and Workforce Committee on **2026-07-21**. **Canada** has an *AI for All* strategy (2026-06-04) and no statute; **Mexico** has bills and no enforcing regulator | 🟢 **Three new placed rows** — `academic-innovation/django-lti` (Michigan, MIT, pushed today), `learningequality/morango` and `le-utils` (MIT), `cs341-illinois/coursebook` (NCSA + CC-BY, 3 839 ★). Supply here is university- and foundation-shaped, which T46 predicted |
| **EMEA** | 🟢 **The compliance runway is unchanged and remains the clearest "why now" on this shelf**, and this pass adds the institutional layer: the **European Commission and OECD presented an AI Literacy Framework for primary and secondary education on 2026-06-18**, and the Commission has published updated guidance on ethical AI and data use in teaching. **Article 4's AI-literacy duty has bound every deploying organisation — every school — since 2025-02-02** | 🟢 **Four new placed rows, the largest EMEA haul this base has taken:** two UK Department for Education production services (**19 717** and **17 549** commits, both MIT, both pushed today), KIT's autograder, TUM's `Ares2` sandbox. 🔵 Plus two institutional addresses read and declined for being dead (Universitat Oberta de Catalunya, Kennisnet) |
| **APAC** | 🟢 **Education is now named in statute rather than in guidance.** **Vietnam**'s high-risk list includes education — automated assessment and behavioural monitoring — with a six-month grace period for systems already operating before **2026-08-15**; **South Korea**'s Framework Act on AI has been in force since **2026-01-22**; **China** regulates by service; **Japan** promotes rather than penalises; **India** relies on its data-protection law; **Singapore** and **Australia** on voluntary frameworks. 🟡 The Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 reports **lower** support for banning AI in schools across the Asian markets surveyed and **higher** support in Australia and New Zealand | 🟡 **One new placed row** — `zaikaman/SGU-Academic-MCP` (Saigon University, MIT, `+0700` on all 116 commits) — and **two read and declined**: `project-sunbird/sunbird-lms-mw` (MIT, 5 458 commits, dead since 2020, and it identifies India's national stack's **archived generation**) and `IndoNLP/nusa-crowd` (Apache-2.0, 293 ★, dormant 28 months) |
| **LATAM** | 🟢 **The regional institution now exists and is education-specific.** UNESCO's **Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean launched 2026-04-14 in Santiago**, with CAF, CENIA (Chile), CETIC.br (Brazil), ECLAC, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo and Fundación Ceibal (Uruguay) named as partners. The adoption denominator from UNESCO IESALC — **200 institutions across 19 countries, 87 % using AI in at least one area, 26 % with a formal AI strategy** — is the sharpest demand figure this base holds for any region. **Uruguay** was the first in the region to sign the Council of Europe's AI Framework Convention; **Colombia** has CONPES 4144 (2025-02); **Peru** has regulatory sandboxes; **Mexico**'s federal bill is still a bill | 🟢 **One new placed row** — `CaioCastro1/usp-mcp` (Universidade de São Paulo systems, MIT, 18 ★, `-0300` on all 378 commits), bringing this base's instrument-placed LATAM count to **four**. 🔵 Plus `agn3si/ResultadosUNAM---data` (MIT, `astro.unam.mx` in the domain channel ⇒ **Mexico**) read and declined on bench size: a real institutional placement on a 13-commit repository |

🟢 **The structural result is the one to carry forward: four legs, four regions, and for the first time a placed supply row in every one.** 🔵 p343 recorded a single instrument-placed LATAM row and nine consecutive zero-supply passes; three passes later the count is four LATAM, and the mechanism was never a better query — **it was reading this base's own 528-address orphan inventory with an institutional filter.**

### 🟡 The 87 % / 26 % gap is the most sellable number in this section, and it is not a technology gap

🔵 UNESCO IESALC's reading — **87 % of surveyed Latin American and Caribbean higher-education institutions use AI somewhere, 26 % have a formal AI strategy** — says adoption is being driven by individual academics, researchers and students rather than institutionally. 🟢 **That 61-point gap is a governance engagement, not a build**: the institution already has the tools in the building and no policy, no inventory, no risk classification and no procurement position. 🔵 It is the same shape the EMEA runway creates by statute (Article 4 literacy already binding, Annex III duties at `2027-12-02`) and the same shape APAC's statutes create in Vietnam and Korea — **three regions arriving at the same first deliverable from three different directions.** 🟡 And it is cheap to staff relative to a platform build, which makes it the natural first engagement in a region where **under a third of pilots reach a P&L**, as this shelf's earlier passes recorded.

### 🔴 Market sizing remains unusable, and this pass can say why in one line

🔵 The legs returned, for the same year: **$6.4 B (IMARC 2025 base, → $79.6 B by 2034)**, **$7.52 B → $10.6 B for 2026 (The Business Research Company, 40.9 % CAGR)**, **$10.6 B → $42.48 B by 2030**, **$136.79 B by 2035**, and **$2.85 B for APAC alone**. 🔴 **A ten-fold disagreement between vendor estimates is not a range, it is an absence of a measurement**, and this base has now recorded it for five consecutive passes. 🟢 **What is quotable instead is a denominator with a method behind it** — 200 institutions, 19 countries, 87 %/26 % — and this pass adds a second kind: **a commit channel.** 19 717 commits and 122 authors on one national service is a defensible statement about what education software costs, and no analyst published it.

| **LATAM — policy** | 🟢 RETRIEVABLE | 🟢 **STRENGTHENED and now measured:** UNESCO IESALC, 200 institutions across 19 countries — **87% using AI, 26% with a formal strategy** |

Federally, **H.R. 8747** advanced at a **2026-07-21** markup — a funding instrument, not a restriction |
| **APAC — supply** | 🟢 RICHEST | 🟢 **DEEPENED institutionally:** `Coursemology/coursemology2` (MIT, **15 821 c · 86 a**, 12 years, live, Singapore), `DECK6/korean-elementary-learning-map` (Korean 2022 curriculum ontology) |

### 🟡 Why the APAC correction is a lesson about altitude rather than about APAC

🔵 p345 asked for *school-level* rules in China, India and Singapore and correctly found none. 🔴 **But no education deployment in Korea or Vietnam is governed by a school-level rule — it is governed by a national AI statute that names education**, and both of those existed and were dated when p345 called the channel empty. 🟢 **The rule: ask the policy question at the layer that BINDS, and name the layer in the verdict.** A region is not policy-empty because the school ministry is quiet; it is policy-empty when nothing retrievable binds the deployer. 🟡 This is the market-shelf twin of `ACTION ZZ-k`: that action split *supply* from *policy*, and this one splits *policy* by **altitude**.

### 🔴 The structural risk this pass would put in front of a client before any licence question

🟢 **Both new curriculum rows are permissive on code and explicitly withhold the DATA.** `DECK6/korean-elementary-learning-map` grants its ontology and dataset and grants **no rights to the Republic of Korea's official curriculum documents**; p345's `Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil` grants its code and excludes the third-party API data and Brazilian public institutions' marks. 🔵 **Two continents, two passes, same shape** — and the GitHub licence classifier reports `NOASSERTION` on the first, so neither a scanner nor a byte band catches it. 🟡 **For an education engagement the corpus is the risk, not the licence**: budget a data-rights review beside the licence review, and expect the deliverable to be a per-source register.

## Pass 345 · 2026-10-20 — the two questions this shelf has been merging are separated and measured: a region can be supply-rich and policy-empty, and the two gaps need opposite responses

**Evidence:** [`p345-region-policy-supply-asymmetry`](../evidence/p345-region-policy-supply-asymmetry.2026-10-20-p345.tsv) · [`p345-eu-omnibus-date-resolution`](../evidence/p345-eu-omnibus-date-resolution.2026-10-20-p345.tsv) · [`p345-orphan-promotion-probe`](../evidence/p345-orphan-promotion-probe.2026-10-20-p345.tsv).

### 🔴 The defect this pass inherited, and it was this shelf's

🟢 `make hooks` ran for the first time in three passes and `gate-absence` came back **RED with `ABSENCE-REFUTED=3`**, firing on the live `README.md` section — which is **p343's**, because p344 never wrote one — and its claim that the EMEA leg had returned *"nothing on the Middle East or Africa"* while naming its own counter-example in the same sentence. 🔵 **The gate was right and the sentence was not wrong — which is the whole lesson.** It was answering *two* questions with *one* sentence: **is anyone BUILDING here** and **can this base RETRIEVE a rule from here**. 🔴 **Merging them is what made six passes of silence look like coverage.** (And the sentence stayed live for two further passes because p344 shipped no README section — see `ACTION ZZ-l` on `agents/top.md`.) `ACTION ZZ-k` separates them permanently: **every regional leg now reports a supply verdict and a policy verdict, and a region is a "gap" only with respect to one of them, named.**

### 🟢 The four prescribed regional legs, each split into the two verdicts

| Region | Supply | Policy | The asymmetry |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LATAM** | 🟢 **RICH, and it was never read** — `Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil`, MIT, **1 817 ★**, 246 commits, `-0300` on every one, pushed **today**; plus `latam-gpt/anonymization-filter` placed institutionally to `uc.cl` | 🟢 **RETRIEVABLE** — Latam-GPT launched **2026-02-10** by **CENIA** (Chile) with 65 institutions across 15 countries; a **UNESCO–CENIA** partnership signed 2026-03 stands up a **Regional Observatory on AI in Education** serving Ministries of Education | 🔴 **Both verdicts are positive and this shelf published a gap for six passes.** The row was in this base's own 528-address orphan list the whole time |
| **North America** | 🟢 PRESENT — `terracotta-education/terracotta`, Apache-2.0, 2 815 commits, 18 authors, live | 🟢 **RICHEST of the four, and fragmented** — the United States federal layer reports **zero binding AI curriculum standards** as of 2026-05; ~34 states plus DC and Puerto Rico have issued guidance | 🟡 The policy work is per-state, so a national engagement is 50 compliance surfaces, not one |
| **EMEA** | 🟡 **THIN** — `AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark` places to `glasgow.ac.uk` (5/5) on a **5-commit** bench; `EscolaLMS/LRS` places to Poland and died 2024-07 | 🟢 **STRONGEST policy channel this pass** — the education high-risk deadline **moved**, see below | 🟡 Europe is where the rules are legible and the open-source education bench is weakest |
| **EMEA — Middle East and Africa** | 🔴 **ZERO authorship-placed rows** — but the bare form of that claim is refuted below | 🔴 **ZERO retrievable sources this pass** | 🟢 **This is the split that matters**: the building is happening *about* Africa *from* Europe, and the grants are mostly missing |
| **APAC** | 🟢 **RICHEST supply channel** — one topic query returned 12 live rows (`PhysicsOS` 251 ★, `Bloom` 289 ★, `Study-Mate`, `AI_Tutor_Release`, `civil-ai`) | 🔴 **THIN** — the leg returned **zero** school-level rules for China, India or Singapore; Japan shows movement toward official digital textbooks with no AI-specific rule attached | 🔴 **The exact inverse of MEA**, and it needs the opposite response: in APAC this base must buy policy, in MEA it must buy supply |

### 🟢 The Middle East and Africa claim, restated so it is true

🔵 `AI-for-Education` is a **Glasgow-benched** organisation whose portfolio is **aimed at Sub-Saharan Africa**: `luganda-linguistic-benchmarks` (Uganda), `lesson-plan-parse-mbsse` (Sierra Leone's Ministry of Basic and Senior Secondary Education), `gpf-alignment` and `global-proficiency-framework`. 🔴 **`gpf-alignment` and `luganda-linguistic-benchmarks` return HTTP 404 on `LICENSE` — no grant at the root name**, so they are readable and not buildable. 🟡 `lesson-plan-parse-mbsse` (MIT, 1 065 B) and `edu-qurating` (MIT, 1 063 B) carry one, at 1 ★ and 3 ★.

🟢 **So the honest sentence is three sentences.** The work exists. The grants largely do not. The authorship is European and the subject is African — and per `ACTION ZZ-b` only the authorship may enter a `Placed` cell. 🔵 **For an engagement in the region, the actionable half is that the curriculum artefacts** — the Global Proficiency Framework digitisation especially — **are the assets, and a one-email licence request is the cheapest unblocking move on these shelves.**

### 🟢 The EU date this base published as a conflict is now explained — and still not verified

🔵 p344 published the EU AI Act education-enforcement date as **contested (2026-08-02 vs 2026-07-27)** and refused to pick one. 🟢 **That was correct, and both dates are real: they belong to different instruments.**

| Date | Instrument | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-07-24 | **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** — the *Digital Omnibus on AI* | publication in the Official Journal |
| **2026-07-27** | same | **entry into force** — the date p344 saw and could not attribute |
| **2026-08-02** | Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 **as adopted** | the **original** application date for Annex III stand-alone high-risk systems, education among them |
| 2026-08-02 | 2024/1689 **Article 50** | transparency duties — reported **not** moved; **this is the date that still binds an education deployer today** |
| **2027-12-02** | Annex III via Art. 6(2), **including education** | application **deferred**, roughly 16 further months |
| 2028-08-02 | Annex I (AI embedded in regulated products) | deferred |
| **2025-02-02** | Article 5 prohibition | **emotion recognition in education and the workplace — already prohibited, not deferred** |

🔴 **This base does NOT hold these dates as verified, and the reason is environmental, not editorial.** Every primary host was tried and every one returns **HTTP 000** from this session: `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu`, `artificialintelligenceact.eu`, `europa.eu`. 🟢 **What is recorded is a convergence of nine mutually independent secondary sources, one of which (`nicfab.eu`) quotes the Official Journal table directly** — and one outlier (`usercentrics.com`) which gives a signature date of 2026-07-08 and no publication date, recorded so the disagreement is visible. 🟡 **Per `gate-dates` this stays a claim with its channel named until a primary read is possible. No engagement should take a compliance date from this shelf; it should take the QUESTION from this shelf and the date from counsel.**

🟢 **The engagement consequence is the opposite of relaxing.** The deadline moved; **the two things already binding did not.** Emotion recognition in a classroom product is prohibited **now**, and Article 50 transparency applies **now**. 🔵 The deferral buys documentation time for admission scoring, placement and outcome evaluation — the Annex III uses — and buys nothing at all for affect detection.

### 🟡 Market sizing remains unusable, and the reason is worth keeping

🔴 Two sources attribute **incompatible 2026 figures to the same analyst** (HolonIQ): roughly **USD 23 bn at ~42% CAGR**, and **USD 12.3 bn**. 🟢 Published as a conflict, never as a number. 🔵 The adoption percentages in this channel come from vendor and content-marketing pages with self-reported surveys and are directional at best — with one exception worth citing because it is counter-intuitive: the **Digital Education Council Global Survey 2026** reports APAC faculty the **most enthusiastic** (57% excited) while faculty **intent** to use AI fell from **76% (2025) to 67% (2026)**, the lowest of any region. 🟡 **Enthusiasm and intent are moving in opposite directions in this base's richest supply region.**

## Pass 344 · 2026-10-20 — the eight-pass demand saturation BREAKS: all four regional legs returned education-specific regulation, and one returned the first hard adoption denominator this base has held for LATAM

**Evidence:** [`p344-region-placement`](../evidence/p344-region-placement.2026-10-20-p344.tsv) · [`p344-dst-window-inconclusive`](../evidence/p344-dst-window-inconclusive.2026-10-20-p344.tsv) · [`p344-address-resolution`](../evidence/p344-address-resolution.2026-10-20-p344.tsv).

🔴 **`make hooks` could not be run (tool policy, `Code from External`) — second consecutive pass.** `ACTION ZZ-a` satisfied; no instrument-derived cell below.

### 🟢 The four prescribed regional demand legs, leg by leg — and the eighth consecutive saturation ends here

🔵 `p342` recorded the **eighth** consecutive saturation and `p343` found three of four legs returning governance rather than market. 🟢 **This pass all four returned education-specific substance, the first time since p334, and three of them returned a DATE that changes how an engagement is scoped.**

- 🟢 **EMEA — the regulatory clock moved again, and in the direction that buys time.** **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** (July 2026) pushes the compliance deadline for **stand-alone high-risk education systems to 2027-12-02**. 🔴 **Article 50 transparency duties are live NOW — since 2026-08-02** — and the AI Office plus national authorities began enforcing on that date. Schools are **deployers** under the Act, which puts human-oversight and transparency duties on the institution, not only on the vendor. 🟡 **Sources disagree on the amendment's exact entry-into-force date** (Commission page: 27 July; a secondary newsletter: published 24 July, in force three days later) — **the 2027-12-02 deadline and the 2026-08-02 transparency date are the two that matter and both are consistent across sources.**
- 🟢 **APAC — two binding education-specific regimes, and one is the most explicit in the world.** 🔴 **Vietnam's AI Law lists education among its high-risk systems by name — automated assessment and behavioural monitoring — with compliance due 2027-03-01**, plus a six-month grace period for education systems already operating before **2026-08-15**. 🟢 **Korea's Framework Act on AI has been in force since 2026-01-22**, carrying high-impact and generative-AI duties and a **local-representative requirement for large foreign firms** — which is a direct constraint on how Globant would contract into Korea. 🔵 China regulates service-by-service (recommendation algorithms, deep synthesis, generative AI, content labelling); Japan, Singapore, India and Australia remain promotion-or-guidance regimes.
- 🟢 **North America — the volume is now the story, and it is state-level.** **134 AI-in-education bills introduced across 31 states in 2026**, concentrated on data privacy, classroom-use limits and curriculum. 🟢 **Four states — Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma, Virginia — now require BOTH state guidance and mandatory district-level AI policies**, and **Ohio's district-policy mandate had a 2026-07-01 deadline**. 🔴 **New York City's guidance bars AI from grading, discipline and placement decisions outright** — the three uses the EU AI Act classifies as high-risk, prohibited by a district rather than deferred to 2027. 🔴 **The amended COPPA Rule reached its general compliance date on 2026-04-22**, which binds any child-directed service. 🟡 **No Canada or Mexico coverage in this leg — an informed gap, re-measured.**
- 🟢 **LATAM — the first hard adoption denominator this base has ever held for the region.** **UNESCO IESALC with UNU-IAS, 200 institutions across 19 countries: 87% use AI somewhere in their operations, and only 26% have a formal AI strategy.** 🔴 **That 61-point gap IS the regional opportunity, stated as a number for the first time.** Adoption is driven by **individual faculty and students rather than institutional policy**, and **private institutions have materially more planning and governance capacity than public ones.** 🟢 **UNESCO launched the Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean on 2026-04-14** — partners **CAF, CENIA (Chile), ECLAC, Fundación Santillana, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo and Fundación Ceibal (Uruguay)** — with a Mexico pilot building centres of excellence with **CONALEP and DGETI**. 🟡 **Regulation remains general-purpose, not education-specific:** Uruguay first in the region to sign the Council of Europe AI convention (2025), Peru's phased framework, Colombia's CONPES 4144 (Feb 2025), Mexico's federal bill in the Senate, Brazil's and Chile's moving at different speeds.

### 🔴 The supply side: tenth consecutive measurement of zero new placeable slugs, and the contamination is now the finding
