## Opportunities by region

### North America


- 🟢 **Added p351 — scope the engagement TEACHER-FACING and administrative, because the student-facing door is closing by district.** **Katy ISD** bans generative-AI chatbots for **K-6**; **New York City** runs a **one-year moratorium on student-facing AI through eighth grade**, on top of the grading, discipline, placement and IEP prohibitions. The demand figures are teacher-side too: **60 %** of US K-12 teachers used AI in 2024-25, **32 %** weekly. 🔴 **Quote bill counts as INTRODUCED, not enacted** — **134 bills across 31 states** in 2026, against only **four states** that enacted an AI-in-education law in all of 2025. New federally: **H.R. 8747**, the K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026, advanced in committee **July 2026** (🟡 not enacted, no floor date).
- 🟢 **Added p345 — the responsible-experiment seam, and it is a differentiator rather than a feature.** `terracotta-education/terracotta` (**Apache-2.0**, 2 815 commits, 18 authors, live 2026-10-19, `unicon.net` + `iu.edu` + `calpoly.edu`) delivers randomised controlled trials **inside live coursework** over LTI 1.3, with consent collected in a form the instructor cannot see and non-consenting students filtered out of exports. 🔵 **The opportunity:** every US district and university buying an AI tutor this year is being asked by a board or an IRB to show it worked. ~34 states plus DC and Puerto Rico have issued AI guidance and the federal layer reports **zero** binding curriculum standards, so the burden of proof is institutional — and an engagement that ships the evidence layer with the tutor wins the renewal. See `compose/patterns.md` **`P31`**. 🟡 **Named statutes to design against:** Idaho SB 1227 (signed 2026-03-19, effective 2026-07-01), Ohio (district AI policy due 2026-07-01), Oklahoma SB 1734 (policy before 2027-28), SUNY AI literacy in general education from Fall 2026.

🔴 **The sellable gap is governance, and it is ~56 points wide.** ChatGPT used by **66%** of students; **only 10%** of institutions have formal AI guidelines; **71%** of US teachers report no AI training *(search channel, vendor roundup — attributed, not asserted)*.

- 🟢 **AI acceptable-use and assessment-redesign engagements.** No model ships this. The deliverable is policy + training + an audit trail, and the buyer already has the usage problem.
- 🟡 **The regulatory surface is a state patchwork, not a federal rule** — Colorado and Texas have introduced piecemeal requirements; there is no single rulebook. 🔵 **Sell per-state compliance mapping, not "AI Act readiness."**
- 🔴 **Any NA vendor selling into Europe inherits the EU AI Act's high-risk classification for education.** That is a cross-border design constraint on a US-built product, and it is cheapest to honour at architecture time.
- 🟢 **Build-on tier for closed IP:** `OpenOLAT` (Apache-2.0, 10 982 B) and `JupyterHub` (BSD, 1 475 B) — the two platforms whose grant does not reach your layer.
- 🟢 **Added p343 — the mandates are no longer all prospective, and three of them change what you BUILD, not just what you file.** **New York City** places **grading and discipline** in a *prohibited* tier, so an NA K-12 product must be able to demonstrate that a human decision sits between the model and the record. **California AB 1159** would bar using **student data to train AI models** — a constraint on the training pipeline, not on the UI, and the one most likely to invalidate an architecture that already exists. **SUNY** has adopted a systemwide policy across **all 64 campuses**, with **AI literacy in general education from fall 2026** — a single procurement surface covering a whole state system. **Alabama H.B. 329** makes a CS course including AI instruction a **graduation requirement**, which is the curriculum-content buy rather than the governance one.
- 🔴 **Added p343 — this subsection is US-only and must be read that way.** The prescribed leg returned **no substantive Canadian source**. 🟢 **Written down rather than left as apparent coverage:** a Canadian engagement cannot be planned off this subsection, and a future pass should run a provincial leg (Ontario, Québec, British Columbia) — because *"North America"* is a vendor sales region and no ministry describes itself with the word. That is `ACTION ZR`'s lesson applied to the other side of the border.

- 🟢 **Added p344 — the state surface now has a volume number and four hard mandates, which turns this from a narrative into a pipeline.** **134 AI-in-education bills introduced across 31 states in 2026.** **Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma and Virginia** require **both** state guidance **and** mandatory district-level AI policies; **Ohio's district mandate deadline was 2026-07-01 and has passed.** 🔴 **Every district under those mandates owes a written policy, an inventory of AI uses and an oversight record, and almost none has the staff to produce one** — a repeatable, scoped engagement with a date already behind it.
- 🔴 **Added p344 — the amended COPPA Rule has been binding since 2026-04-22** on any child-directed service. Together with NYC's prohibition on AI in **grading, discipline and placement**, the region's K-12 constraint set is now as concrete as EMEA's and arrives sooner.
- 🟢 **Added p344 — the Article 14-shaped human-confirmation control is a NA requirement too, and it already ships as MIT source.** `psychquant/che-ntucool-mcp` gates grade and announcement writes behind explicit human confirmation — the same component answers NYC's prohibition, Korea's Act and Vietnam's classification.
- 🔴 **Added p344 — the sharpest grant-absence specimen yet is US-placed.** `loyaniu/moodle-mcp` is institutionally benched (`calvin.edu`, with a genuine `rwth-aachen.de` EMEA co-contribution) and ships **no licence payload and no manifest declaration** ⇒ all rights reserved. **The most credible-looking connector in the region is the one that cannot be built on.**

- 🟢 **Added p346 — the Canadian gap `p343` wrote down is now PARTLY closed, and the answer is that there is no statute to comply with yet.** Canada has **no federal AI statute**; its **"AI for All" strategy launched 2026-06-04** commits to a national AI-literacy push with **no legislative date confirmed** *(search channel, attributed)*. 🔵 **Commercially that is the opposite of a blocker:** a Canadian engagement is a literacy-and-governance build with no conformity exercise attached, and it can reuse the EMEA transparency instrumentation without the Annex III documentation. 🟡 **Mexico is the inverse of the US:** a stack of federal bills converging on an unacceptable/high/lower-risk taxonomy and **no regulator standing to enforce them** — design against the taxonomy, not against an enforcement date.

- 🟡 **Added p346 — the federal layer finally has a bill worth naming, and it is a FUNDING instrument rather than a restriction.** The **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act (H.R. 8747)** was advanced by the House Education and Workforce Committee at a **2026-07-21 markup**, largely along party lines, and would let schools spend **federal funds** on AI curriculum *(search channel; its Senate path is unresolved and this base does not predict it)*. 🟢 **Paired with the 2025 executive order's pledge — now carrying commitments from 200+ companies and organisations — the US K-12 motion is money arriving for curriculum, against a state patchwork restricting USE.** 🔵 Sell curriculum and teacher-capability build to the funding side, and policy/audit work to the restricting side; they are different buyers in the same district. 🟡 **Maryland's districts owe their own AI policies within 120 days of the state department's guidance**, which is the kind of deadline that makes the governance engagement procurable rather than aspirational.

- 🟢 **Added p346 — three live NA platform benches promoted from this base's own orphan list, all MIT, none previously offered.** [`AnubisLMS/Anubis`](https://github.com/AnubisLMS/Anubis) (**382 ★**, 1 638 commits, NYU, per-student containerised IDE + autograder), [`openedx/tutor-contrib-aspects`](https://github.com/openedx/tutor-contrib-aspects) (**Apache-2.0**, 2 298 commits, pushed today — the learning-analytics layer for an Open edX estate), and [`openwallet-foundation-labs/learner-credential-wallet`](https://github.com/openwallet-foundation-labs/learner-credential-wallet) (**89 ★**, 1 309 commits, verifiable learner credentials, now a **Linux Foundation** lab). 🔵 **Why it matters for a NA engagement:** the autograder and the analytics plug-in are the two components a university asks for after the tutor pilot, and both are now permissive rows with benches rather than aspirations. See `compose/patterns.md` **`P33`**.

- 🟢 **Added p347 — three new placed NA rows, and the biggest was invisible to this base's licence channel.** [`cs341-illinois/coursebook`](https://github.com/cs341-illinois/coursebook) (**3 839 ★**, 736 c, 42 a, live) publishes a deliberate three-way grant — **NCSA** for code, **CC-BY-3.0** for remixed content, **CC-BY-4.0** for PDF builds — inside a **directory** named `LICENSE`, which 404s every ladder rung. Plus `academic-innovation/django-lti` (Michigan, MIT, pushed 2026-10-20) and **`learningequality/morango`** (MIT) — Kolibri's offline **sync protocol**, which this base had shelved the product of and never the protocol. 🟡 **Read Michigan's `django-lti`, not the Harvard-named fork of it**, whose own commit channel is `umich.edu`.
- 🟡 **Added p347 — the US policy surface now has a volume number for the 2026 session and it is still sub-federal.** **134 AI-in-education bills across 31 states** (search-channel convergence), with **California AB 1159** barring student data from model training and Oklahoma and Maryland requiring human oversight on high-stakes decisions; **H.R. 8747** (K-12 AI literacy funding) was reported out of committee **2026-07-21**. **Canada**: an *AI for All* strategy dated 2026-06-04 and no statute. **Mexico**: bills, no enforcing regulator.

- 🟢 **Added p348 — the EFFICACY seam is now a component, and it reframes the pitch.** [`terracotta-education/terracotta`](https://github.com/terracotta-education/terracotta) (**Apache-2.0**, 2 815 c, **14 author names**, pushed 2026-10-19, 21 ★) runs randomised controlled trials inside live LMS courses over LTI, with privacy protections for participants. 🔵 **Every AI-in-education proposal contains an efficacy claim and almost none contain a design that could test one.** Pre-registering an experiment alongside the tutoring build costs an LTI integration, not a research department — and it is the cheapest differentiator on this shelf.
- 🟢 **Added p348 — the outcome number this region was missing.** [`datakind/student-success-tool`](https://github.com/datakind/student-success-tool) (**MIT**, 3 015 c, 25 authors, holder `DataKind`, Google.org-funded) is an advising-triage pipeline whose README reports **John Jay College's CUSP programme at +32 % senior graduation over two years**. 🔵 **A named institution, a named programme and a two-year window is the most citable "does it work" claim on these shelves** — quote it as the client's own benchmark, and note the tool has been dormant since 2025-09-08.
- 🟡 **Added p348 — the credentials layer changed STEWARD, and that is the question to ask before the licence question.** [`digitalcredentials/learner-credential-wallet`](https://github.com/digitalcredentials/learner-credential-wallet) (**MIT**, 89 ★, 1 309 c, 33 authors) resolves to the **same HEAD `1c46a82ba384`** as [`openwallet-foundation-labs/learner-credential-wallet`](https://github.com/openwallet-foundation-labs/learner-credential-wallet): the MIT Digital Credentials Consortium has handed the reference wallet to the **OpenWallet Foundation**, and its README calls the latest release the *"last release as the Digital Credentials Consortium at MIT"*.
- 🔴 **Added p348 — the state surface has three different volumes and opposite poles enacted in one session.** 134 / 77 / 52 bills across 31 / 27 / 25 states depending on the tracker; **California AB 1159** bars student data from model training; **Oklahoma SB 1734** requires human-in-the-loop and bars AI as the main basis for grading or discipline, with a parental opt-out; **Alabama HB 329** makes an AI-inclusive CS course a graduation requirement; **Maryland SB 720** requires district policies and AI coordinators; **Ohio** set **July 1** for every district to adopt one. **New York A.9190** would ban classroom AI below grade 9 while **Utah SB 322** builds a red-team sandbox. 🔵 **Sell per-state compliance mapping; there is no national rulebook to be ready for.**

### EMEA


- 🔴 **Added p351 — `p350`'s "live deadline" framing is WITHDRAWN, and the replacement is NEARER rather than softer.** The **Annex III** education deadline is **2027-12-02**, not 2026-08-02: Regulation (EU) **2026/1744** (*Digital Omnibus on AI*), OJ **2026-07-24**, in force **2026-07-27**; Annex I products **2028-08-02**. `p345` had this right. 🟢 **But Article 50 transparency was NOT deferred and its grace period for marking content from already-deployed systems ends 2026-12-02** — so sell the **Article 50 transparency and content-marking kit first** (recipe `P41`), the **Annex III conformance programme second**. **Article 27**'s FRIA is **unchanged**. 🟡 Sources **disagree on Article 4** (AI literacy), published as disputed. The **10 % of 450+ institutions with formal AI guidelines** is still the gap, and against a six-week deadline it is a sharper sell. 🔴 Every date here is **secondary-sourced**: EU primary hosts are `connect_rejected` at this session's egress proxy.
- 🟢 **Added p345 — sell the re-ordering, not the reprieve.** Regulation (EU) **2026/1744** defers **Annex III** education high-risk from 2026-08-02 to **2027-12-02** (secondary-source convergence; **every EU primary host returns HTTP 000 from this environment**, so the date travels as a claim with its channel named). 🔴 **Two obligations did not move:** emotion recognition in education has been **prohibited since 2025-02-02**, and Article 50 transparency applies from 2026-08-02. 🔵 **The engagement, concretely:** an affect-feature audit billable **now** (attention scoring, engagement detection and affect-aware tutoring are out, deferral or not), and an Annex III documentation programme that can be scheduled across 2027 instead of crashed. 🟡 **The supply-side caveat for delivery:** this region's best-placed new row is a **5-commit** bench (`AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark`, MIT, `glasgow.ac.uk`), so European engagements should expect to import their implementation bench and keep the local contribution in governance and evaluation.

🔴 **This region returned NOTHING education-specific on the prescribed leg, and that is written down rather than left as apparent coverage.** Every result was enterprise AI or general EU AI Act commentary; one of two substantive sources is a **2023** study. No edtech vendor, no ministry of education, no school-level rule, no student-data material.

- 🟡 **What stands, because it is the one named binding instrument:** the **EU AI Act classifies education AI as high-risk.** EMEA is the only region where **regulation leads adoption** — the inverse of everywhere else.
- 🟢 **That inversion is itself the opportunity:** conformity-by-construction. Documented data lineage, human-in-the-loop on anything affecting grades or progression, and a technical file that exists before the audit does.
- 🟢 **The supply side here is genuinely strong and already on these shelves** — H5P (Norway), `openpatch/hyperbook`, `otacke/h5p-theme-picker` (Germany), OpenOLAT (Zurich). **EMEA is this KB's deepest permissive authoring stack**, which makes the thin policy reading the more frustrating.
- 🔵 **`ACTION ZR` applies here first:** re-run this region with political names (*"AI in schools Europe regulation 2026"*, *"EU AI Act education high-risk"*), because `EMEA` is a vendor sales region and no ministry describes itself with the word.
- 🟡 **Added p343 — the AI Act's education-enforcement date is CONTESTED across sources, and this base publishes neither.** One has the Commission's AI Office and national authorities enforcing **from 2026-08-02**; another has amendments adopted **June 2026** and in force **2026-07-27**. 🔵 **Sell against the obligations, which are not in dispute** — risk management, data governance, human oversight and conformity assessment before deployment, for admissions, student evaluation and exam scoring — **and let the client's counsel own the date.**
- 🟢 **Added p343 — the non-binding instrument is the cheapest way in.** The Commission's **updated ethical guidelines for educators on AI and data** are explicitly motivated by *"the significant increase of AI usage in education and the introduction of regulations like the AI Act."* A guidelines-shaped deliverable is procurable without a high-risk conformity exercise, and is the natural engagement ahead of one.
- 🔴 **Added p343 — the MEA half of this region is a POLICY gap, not a supply gap, and the two were being merged.** The leg returned nothing on the Middle East or Africa for another pass. 🟢 **But `ahmedEid1/lumen` (GPL-3.0, 828 commits, 3 authors) carries `+0200` on all 828 commits *including 39 outside Mar–Nov* — a non-shifting UTC+2 zone, i.e. the MEA band, not CEST.** There are builders here; there are no retrievable rules. 🟡 **A band is not a country:** this does not distinguish South Africa from Egypt, and this pass does not claim to.
- 🟡 **Added p343 — do not quote the regional market size.** A $2.64B (2026) → $8.0B (2030) at 31.9% figure circulates for European education AI from a low-authority aggregator. `ACTION ZQ` holds: **attribute or omit.**

- 🟢 **Added p344 — the Annex III clock moved again and the split is now the whole EMEA pitch.** **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** (July 2026) moves stand-alone high-risk **education** systems to **2027-12-02**. 🔴 **Article 50 transparency duties are live since 2026-08-02 and are being enforced** by the AI Office and national authorities. 🟢 **So: sell transparency and oversight instrumentation NOW, and scope the Annex III conformity work against 2027.** 🟡 Sources disagree on the amendment's exact entry-into-force date; the **2027-12-02** and **2026-08-02** dates are consistent across all of them and are the two to quote.
- 🟢 **Added p344 — schools are DEPLOYERS**, carrying human-oversight and transparency duties themselves. **That is a governance-tooling engagement that does not require replacing any platform** — the cheapest possible entry into an institution.
- 🟡 **Added p344 — new EMEA supply, with the limits that decide its use.** `formalms/formalms` (**Forma LMS**) — a 13-year Italian corporate-training LMS, **7 494 commits · 59 authors**, real vendor bench (`joint-tech.it`, `grifomultimedia.it`), **0% agent-authored**, placed **EMEA — Italy** on a CET/CEST pair. 🔴 **It is GPL-2.0 and three months stale, and a secondary source called it Apache-2.0 — the payload at `html/license.txt` refutes that.** Useful only where copyleft is already accepted; **not a permissive foundation.**

- 🟢 **Added p346 — this region's SUPPLY verdict is REVERSED, and it was reversed by reading this base's own orphan list rather than by a new search.** p345 recorded EMEA supply as **THIN** on the strength of a 5-commit bench and advised European engagements to *import* their implementation bench. 🔴 **That advice is now wrong, and the counter-evidence was already in this repository.** Three live, institutionally-benched EMEA rows, each grant read at the pin: [`oaknational/oak-open-curriculum-ecosystem`](https://github.com/oaknational/oak-open-curriculum-ecosystem) (**MIT**, **6 832 commits · 15 authors**, live 2026-10-19 — API, SDK **and an MCP app** over the Oak Open Curriculum data, GMT/BST confirmed); [`DFE-Digital/education-benchmarking-and-insights`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/education-benchmarking-and-insights) (**MIT**, **5 266 commits · 35 authors**, pushed today, **`education.gov.uk`** the second-modal author domain — the **UK Department for Education's** own school-benchmarking service); and [`SafeExamBrowser/seb-win-refactoring`](https://github.com/SafeExamBrowser/seb-win-refactoring) (**355 ★**, 1 545 commits, live, **ETH Zurich**, CET/CEST confirmed). 🟡 **The grant caveat on the third:** it is **MPL-2.0**, not MIT or Apache — file-level reciprocity, so modifications to its own files travel back while a larger work around it does not. 🟢 **The honest new sentence: EMEA is where the rules are clearest AND where a national-curriculum data layer, a government benchmarking service and a university exam-delivery client are all available permissively.** Europe no longer needs to import the bench; it needs to be told the bench exists.

- 🟡 **Added p346 — a fourth EMEA row with the EU's own taxonomy in it, and a bench small enough to say so.** [`KonstantinosPetrakis/esco-skill-extractor`](https://github.com/KonstantinosPetrakis/esco-skill-extractor) (**32 ★**, MIT — **untitled payload**, body verbatim) extracts **ESCO skills and ISCO occupations** from job descriptions and CVs, placed **EMEA — Greece** on a confirmed EET/EEST shift; **29 commits, dormant 16 months.** 🔵 **ESCO is the European Commission's own skills classification, which makes this the cheapest bridge between a curriculum and the EU skills framework a client is already reporting against** — and at 29 commits it is a component to adopt and maintain, not a dependency to rely on. 🟢 **The 1EdTech read that digital credentials become a core skills-and-hiring mechanism lands here**: credential wallet (Global) + ESCO extraction (EMEA) + curriculum ontology is a full skills-passport stack across three shelves.

- 🔵 **Added p346 — the OECD's 2026 Digital Education Outlook argues for purpose-built educational AI over general-purpose tools** *(search channel, attributed)*, which is the same conclusion this base reached from the supply side: the rows that survive adjudication here are curriculum-, assessment- and consent-shaped, not chat-shaped. 🟡 A **£4 million UK commitment to AI lesson-planning and marking tools** circulates from a low-authority aggregator — **attribute or omit**, per `ACTION ZQ`. 🔴 **The MEA half of this region returned nothing retrievable on the policy channel again this pass, and the supply channel was not re-run for it** — so no verdict either way is published for the Middle East and Africa beyond p345's, which stands: builders present, grants largely missing, rules unretrievable.

- 🟢 **Added p347 — the largest EMEA supply haul this base has taken, and two of the four rows are a NATIONAL GOVERNMENT in production.** [`DFE-Digital/teaching-vacancies`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/teaching-vacancies) (**MIT, 19 717 commits, 122 authors**, pushed 2026-10-20) and [`DFE-Digital/publish-teacher-training`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/publish-teacher-training) (**MIT, 17 549 commits, 142 authors**, pushed 2026-10-20) are the UK Department for Education's live services. 🟢 **This is the citable answer to *"what does a compliant national education service cost to build and run"*** — and it is MIT, so a studio may read it, fork it and quote it to a ministry. Plus KIT's `kit-sdq/autograder` (MIT at `LICENSE.md`) and TUM's [`ls1intum/Ares2`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Ares2) — the **sandbox for executing untrusted student code** that every autograding row on these shelves needed and none had.
- 🟡 **Added p347 — the non-binding layer gained an instrument worth naming in a bid.** The **European Commission and OECD presented an AI Literacy Framework for primary and secondary education on 2026-06-18**, which is the cheapest hook for an Article 4 literacy engagement: the duty has bound every deploying organisation — every school — since **2025-02-02**, while the Annex III education duties sit at **2027-12-02**. 🔴 **Still search-channel convergence, not a verified read:** every EU primary domain fails on both fetch channels from this environment ([`p347-policy-channel-reachability`](../evidence/p347-policy-channel-reachability.2026-10-20-p347.tsv)).

- 🟢 **Added p348 — the INTEGRITY layer, and it is the question a faculty board asks first.** [`dodona-edu/dolos`](https://github.com/dodona-edu/dolos) (**MIT**, 353 ★, 3 859 c, 25 author names, pushed 2026-10-19) is source-code plagiarism detection from **Ghent University**, placed by **three independent channels agreeing**: the holder line `Team Dolos <dolos@ugent.be>`, `2080×+0200` CET/CEST, and the owner. 🔵 **In an EMEA higher-ed procurement, *"maintained by a Belgian university"* is an answer and *"maintained by a GitHub account"* is not.**
- 🟢 **Added p348 — a THIRD live UK national education service, and the best cost evidence this base holds.** [`DFE-Digital/education-benchmarking-and-insights`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/education-benchmarking-and-insights) — **MIT**, holder line **`Department for Education - Digital`**, **5 266 commits · 30 authors · pushed on the day of this pass · 4 ★**. *"Compare school metrics against similar schools."* 🔵 **Read the star count against the commit count before quoting either**: this is a running national service with the GitHub profile of a hobby project, and the pairing is the argument for sizing an engagement off commits and authors rather than attention.
- 🔴 **Added p348 — Annex III needs FAIRNESS EVIDENCE and this base had never named the library.** The high-risk duty for admissions, assessment and proctoring at **2027-12-02** turns on demonstrated non-discrimination across protected groups, and a technical file that asserts it without measuring it fails an audit. [`fairlearn/fairlearn`](https://github.com/fairlearn/fairlearn) (**MIT**, 2 300 ★, 1 117 c, **139 author names**, pushed today) produces that measurement. 🟡 **It carries no education token, which is why every education-token filter here was blind to it** — and so is blind to the rest of the compliance tier.
- 🟡 **Added p348 — the non-binding instrument is now specific enough to build a service on.** **AILit** (European Commission + OECD, presented **2026-06-17**) defines AI literacy for primary and secondary schooling in **four domains and nineteen competences**, built with CodeAI from a 2 000+ contributor consultation, and will inform **PISA 2029**. Article 4's AI-literacy duty has bound every AI-using organisation — therefore every school — since **2025-02-02**, and an **EACEA** squad maps Member-State implementation **2026-10 → 2027-01**. 🔴 Sources **conflict** on whether the Digital Omnibus altered Article 4; this base publishes neither reading.
- 🟢 **Added p348 — the Africa half of this region has an instrument, and it is a low-resource-language one.** [`AI-for-Education/voice-ai-evaluation-framework`](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/voice-ai-evaluation-framework) (**MIT**, holder `Fab AI Foundation`, 61 c, 4 authors, `+0200`/`+0300`) evaluates ASR for **early-grade reading assessment in Kiswahili child speech** — the only low-resource-language education evaluation harness on these shelves, from a UK consultancy whose other repos name **Sierra Leone's Ministry of Basic and Senior Secondary Education**. 🔵 This is what AI-for-education tooling in a low-resource setting actually looks like: evaluation harnesses and data curation, not tutors.

### APAC


- 🟢 **Added p351 — the supply hole is CLOSED, and the licence is the whole brief.** Two education-specific rows, both **placed by commit stream** — the first pass to place two in this region by instrument. [`haolpku/K12-KGraph`](https://github.com/haolpku/K12-KGraph) (Peking University + HUST, `.edu.cn`, **NeurIPS 2026 Evaluations & Datasets Track**) is the better dataset and is 🔴 **`CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0` — NON-COMMERCIAL**, so it is **unusable for a client build**; its checkpoints are **Apache-2.0** ×2 and **Llama 3.1 Community** (not OSI), and whether the NC arm reaches the weights is **unsettled — counsel, not this base**. [`aclascollege/polymath`](https://github.com/aclascollege/polymath) is **ODbL + CC-BY-SA and usable**, but it is **94 courses**: a **qualification-framework crosswalk** (ISCED-F 2013, CIP 2020, EQF, AQF), **not a catalogue**. 🔴 Regulation is **general-purpose only for the fourth consecutive pass** — Korea 2026-01-22, Vietnam 2026-03-01, **Taiwan's AI Basic Act passed December 2025**, China binding (penalties to ¥50 M), Japan and Singapore guidance-only. **No national education-ministry AI policy surfaced.**
- 🟢 **Added p345 — the richest supply channel on these shelves, and the thinnest governance one.** One GitHub topic query returned **12 live rows**, among them `122-syf-wcy/PhysicsOS` (251 ★, a K-12 physics agent driving a real physics engine so a problem becomes an inspectable scene), `Li-Evan/Bloom` (289 ★, placed APAC — China at p343), `Miaotofu01/Study-Mate`, `Zenglian990/AI_Tutor_Release` and `zhangl1001/civil-ai`. 🔴 **The policy leg returned zero school-level rules for China, India or Singapore**; Japan shows movement toward official digital textbooks with no AI-specific rule attached. 🔵 **So the opportunity is inverted here: the buildable material is local and abundant, and the governance is what a studio supplies.** 🟡 **One counter-intuitive datum worth quoting to a client** (Digital Education Council Global Survey 2026): APAC faculty are the **most enthusiastic** (57% excited) while faculty **intent** to use AI fell **76% → 67%**, the lowest of any region — enthusiasm and intent are diverging, which is a change-management engagement, not a licensing one.

🔴 **Also returned nothing education-specific.** The education-adjacent hits are **corporate L&D** (Pearson/TCS, LearnUpon, Alteryx) — a different buyer from schools and universities. **No national education-ministry AI policy surfaced.**

- 🟡 **The governing posture that did surface is *"sovereign-by-design"***: 2026 is described as the year APAC moves from pilots to production with data residency and sovereignty as the design constraint.
- 🟢 **Read commercially, that favours the self-hostable stack this KB already curates** — Moodle/OpenOLAT/JupyterHub on client infrastructure beats a hosted SaaS tutor in any sovereignty-led procurement. 🔵 **The AGPL §13 trap is the thing to get right**: hosting a *modified* Open edX for a client triggers the source offer, so the boundary is unmodified core + XBlock/LTI.
- 🟡 **A measured adoption constraint, for enterprises rather than schools:** **49%** of APAC businesses cite insufficient real-time data infrastructure.
- 🔴 **Named rows already on these shelves place real APAC supply** — `PerryLink/dsh-essay-rubric-check` (China, `+0800`), `likr/canvas-lms-agent` (Japan, `+0900`), `corsairdev/corsair` (India, `+0530`) — **so the region is not empty of builders; the policy channel is empty of answers.** Those are different gaps and must not be merged.
- 🟢 **Added p343 — one new binding instrument that names education directly: Vietnam's mid-2026 decision on high-risk AI systems covers education, including automated assessment.** 🔵 **That makes Vietnam the second jurisdiction on these shelves, after the EU, where automated assessment is high-risk by statute** — and the cheaper of the two to design for first, because the obligation set is narrower.
- 🟢 **Added p343 — six more APAC rows placed on the commit channel** (`agents/top.md` this pass), and the regional bench is now legible: **China** — `Miaotofu01/Study-Mate` (1.1k ★, **727 commits in five weeks**, `+0800(711)` + `qq.com(197)`) and `Li-Evan/Bloom` (289 ★); **Australia** — `zijinz456/OpenTutor` (`+1100/+1000`, the AEDT/AEST pair); **India** — `karanb192/algo-sensei` (`+0530` on all 9, 🔴 **293 ★ on nine commits, dormant**). 🔵 **APAC is this base's largest regional supply bench by row count and its youngest by history** — most of it is weeks old, so place it as momentum, not as a dependency.
- 🟡 **Added p343 — the exam-prep cluster is this region's distinctive product shape, and it carries a trap worth naming.** `KeWang0622/kaogong-skill` is China civil-service exam preparation whose **authorship** channel reads `-0700/-0500` on five commits, and it resolves to **NOT RESOLVED**. 🔵 **A China-facing product need not have a China-based bench** (`ACTION ZZ-b`), so a regional headcount built from subject matter will overstate this region.

- 🟢 **Added p344 — Vietnam now carries the most explicit education-AI rule in the world, and it has a grandfathering window that is open today.** The Vietnam AI Law names **education** among its high-risk systems — **automated assessment and behavioural monitoring** — with compliance due **2027-03-01**, plus **six months' additional grace for education systems already operating before 2026-08-15.** 🔴 **A system shipped into Vietnam now sits inside that window: a dated, concrete reason to start rather than wait.**
- 🔴 **Added p344 — Korea's Framework Act on AI has been in force since 2026-01-22**, carrying high-impact and generative-AI duties **and a local-representative requirement for large foreign firms.** 🟢 **That is a contracting constraint, not merely a compliance one, and it must reach a Korean proposal before any architecture does.**
- 🟢 **Added p344 — the region's supply is the strongest on these shelves and it just gained the vertical's only live full education ERP.** `openeducat/openeducat_erp` — 🟡 **LGPL-3.0** (read first-hand, `19.0/LICENSE`, 8 241 B), **India**, **1 711 commits · 97 authors, live 2026-10-13**, placed on `techreceptives.com`/`openeducat.com` with `+0530` on 1 493/1 711. 🔴 **Architect it at arm's length: agents outside the ERP, over its API — an Odoo-style addon that subclasses a core model is a modification under the LGPL, not a link.**

- 🟢 **Added p346 — the POLICY verdict is no longer thin, and two binding national statutes now name education.** 🔵 p345 recorded zero retrievable school-level rules here and treated the region as policy-empty; the retrievable layer was the **national-statute** one, which is the layer that actually binds an education deployer. 🔴 **South Korea's AI Basic Act has been in force since 2026-01-22**, covering high-impact AI and carrying generative-AI disclosure duties. 🔴 **Vietnam's AI law is in force from 2026-03-01 and names education among its HIGH-RISK sectors**, with compliance due **2027-03-01** and a **six-month grace period for education systems already operating before 2026-08-15**. 🟡 **China regulates by service type** — recommendation algorithms, deep synthesis, generative AI and content labelling — rather than by sector; **Japan promotes rather than penalises**; **Singapore, India and Australia run voluntary guidance plus data law**, with India's **DPDP** obligations phasing in to **May 2027** and bearing directly on student data *(search channel, attributed)*. 🟢 **So the corrected sentence is precise: school-level rules remain unretrieved, and the national statutes that govern an education deployment are retrievable and dated.** A Korean or Vietnamese engagement has a real compliance surface today; `psychquant/che-ntucool-mcp`'s human-confirmation gate is the component that answers both.

- 🟢 **Added p346 — the deepest APAC bench on these shelves, and it is twelve years old.** [`Coursemology/coursemology2`](https://github.com/Coursemology/coursemology2) — **MIT, 158 ★, 15 821 commits · 86 authors, 2014-07-23 → live** — a coursework platform with assessments, autograded programming questions and submission workflow, placed **APAC — Singapore** on a non-shifting `+0800` (confirmed against 3 628 December–February commits; Singapore has no DST). 🔴 **Its MIT cell does not cover everything it ships:** two submodules, one **Apache-2.0** and one whose grant exists **only in a README**, both named at their pins on `repos/foundations.md`. 🟡 **For a sovereignty-led APAC procurement this is the strongest available answer** — a self-hostable, permissive, maintained coursework platform — provided the vendoring review reads the submodule boundary rather than running a scanner over the superproject.

- 🟡 **Added p346 — a Korean curriculum ontology, and the shape of an education data grant in this region.** [`DECK6/korean-elementary-learning-map`](https://github.com/DECK6/korean-elementary-learning-map) (**116 ★**, 17 commits, new 2026) publishes a learning ontology over the **2022 revised Korean elementary curriculum**, placed **APAC — South Korea** on `+0900`. 🔴 **Its licence reads MIT in the body and `NOASSERTION` to the GitHub classifier**, because a paragraph prepended inside the file grants the code, validators, dataset and ontology while explicitly granting **no rights to the official curriculum source documents**. 🟢 **Second instance of that carve-out in two passes** (after `Mcp-Brasil`), and the lesson for a client is commercial, not legal: **the ontology is buildable, the ministry's source documents are not, and any product that renders the official text needs its own permission.**

- 🟢 **Added p347 — the first APAC agent row placed on the instrument channel since p340, and it is Vietnamese.** [`zaikaman/SGU-Academic-MCP`](https://github.com/zaikaman/SGU-Academic-MCP) — MIT, 116 commits, `+0700` on every one (Vietnam has no DST, so a non-shifting offset is the expected shape, not an untested one) — an MCP server over **Đại học Sài Gòn**'s training portal: timetable, grades, tuition, live GPA simulation. 🟡 Five weeks old, 0 ★, bench stated.
- 🔴 **Added p347 — India's national stack has an ARCHIVED generation, and four addresses in this base's inventory point at it.** `project-sunbird/sunbird-lms-mw` is MIT with **5 458 commits and 53 authors** and its last commit is **2020-05-05**; p346 found `project-sunbird/sunbird-lms-service` diverged from `Sunbird-Lern/lms-service`. 🟢 **Read `Sunbird-Ed` / `Sunbird-Lern` for DIKSHA-era work and treat `project-sunbird` as history** — a 5 458-commit bench is exactly the figure that makes a dead repository look alive.
- 🟡 **Added p347 — education is in statute here, not in guidance, and the dates differ by jurisdiction.** **Vietnam** lists education among high-risk uses (automated assessment, behavioural monitoring) with a six-month grace period for systems operating before **2026-08-15**; **South Korea**'s Framework Act on AI has been in force since **2026-01-22**; **China** regulates by service; **Japan** promotes rather than penalises; **India** leans on its data-protection law; **Singapore** and **Australia** on voluntary frameworks. 🔵 The Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 reports lower support for school AI bans in the Asian markets surveyed and higher support in Australia and New Zealand — **one region, opposite public mandates.**

- 🟡 **Added p348 — one new bench, APAC-engineered, and its own README disqualifies it from production.** [`open-edge-platform/education-ai-suite`](https://github.com/open-edge-platform/education-ai-suite) — **Apache-2.0**, 457 c, 33 authors, pushed on the day of this pass — ships **Smart Classroom** (multimodal session processing, analysis, summarisation) and **Teaching Assistant** (voice-first self-directed study) on OpenVINO for Intel CPU / iGPU / NPU. 🟢 Placement is `196×+0800` + `179×+0530` = **82 % of the commit stream**, so this is an APAC-ENGINEERED suite under a US vendor's org. 🔴 **The README states the applications are for reference and demonstration, NOT production — quote that before the hardware conversation, not after.**
- 🟢 **Added p348 — education is in STATUTE across this region, with hard dates.** Korea's **AI Basic Act** in force **2026-01-22** (the region's first comprehensive mandatory AI law), Vietnam's effective **2026-03-01**, China with no general AI law but enforceable specifics — AI-content labelling and model filing covering **988 services** by 2026-06-30 — and AI reported compulsory from age six. **India makes AI and computational thinking compulsory from Class 3 in the 2026-27 academic year** under the IndiaAI Mission while having no AI statute, governing instead through data-protection rules phasing to **May 2027**. Japan's AI Promotion Act carries no fines and no prohibitions. 🔵 **The engagement shape here is curriculum delivery at national scale under a deadline, not compliance consulting.**
- 🟡 **Added p348 — a placed Indonesian row, and the holder line is what placed it.** [`motiolabs-space/open-academic`](https://github.com/motiolabs-space/open-academic) — **MIT**, holder **`PT Motiolabs Digital Indonesia`**, `+0700` on **all 75** commits, **1 author**, 16 days of work, dormant since 2026-08-23. 🔵 Small, and worth recording precisely because the holder line named the country without a single external lookup.
- 🔴 **Added p348 — public opinion splits inside this region and it affects how a K-12 product is positioned.** Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 finds Asian markets show **lower** support for banning AI in schools than for restricting children's social media, while **Australia and New Zealand show higher** support for a ban. 🟡 ANZ is not a smaller version of the same market.

### LATAM


- 🟢 **Added p351 — the institution-type cut, which names the buyer.** UNESCO IESALC (with UNU-IAS), **200+ institutions across 19 countries**: **87 %** using AI somewhere, **74 %** in teaching and learning, **26 %** with a formal strategy. Broken out: **private non-profit 84 % · public 68 % · private for-profit 52 %.** 🔵 **The public segment carries both the volume and the governance gap**, which is the clearest engagement shape this region has produced here. Second independent denominator: the **Digital Education Council LATAM survey 2026**, **30 000+ respondents across 29 institutions**. **ILIA 3rd edition** (March 2026) finds school-level AI education *"growing but limited to a few exceptions"*. Teacher use: **Uruguay 75 %** (Ceibal), **Brazil 56 % · Chile 55 % · Colombia 53 % · Costa Rica 52 %** against an **OECD average of 36 %** (TALIS 2024). 🔴 **Supply rows this pass: still ZERO.** `p348`'s reversal stands — LATAM will not grow a platform tier — and `p350`'s **66 : 1** recogniser asymmetry is **carried, not re-measured**.
- 🟢 **Added p345 — the region this shelf called barren for six passes produced the single largest new row of the pass, and it was in this base's own orphan list.** `Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil` (**MIT code grant, 1 072 B stock**, **1 817 ★**, 301 forks, 246 commits, `-0300` on all of them, pushed 2026-10-20) exposes **INEP** — ENEM microdata and the national school census — plus `FNDE`, through an MCP server over ~70 Brazilian public APIs, with a **PII column whitelist in code**, re-identification **VEDADO** under LGPD art. 12, and formal microdata research routed out to INEP's **SEDAP** service. 🔴 **Its grant is a split cell:** MIT covers the code; `NOTICE` (3 135 B), `ACCEPTABLE_USE.md` (8 109 B) and `SOURCES.md` (19 286 B, 61 headed sections) scope the data, the institutional marks and the permitted uses. 🔵 **The demand side matches:** Latam-GPT launched **2026-02-10** (CENIA, Chile — 65 institutions across 15 countries) and a **UNESCO–CENIA** partnership signed 2026-03 stands up a **Regional Observatory on AI in Education** serving Ministries of Education. 🟢 **The engagement is `compose/patterns.md` `P32`: a defensible-on-day-one agent over national assessment data, 6–8 weeks**, with the four hard rules taken from the project's own documents. 🟡 Also placed this pass but **not** recommended as a bench: `latam-gpt/anonymization-filter` (MIT, `uc.cl`, Chile) — 6 commits, dormant 17 months.

🟢 **The richest region this pass, and the only one with a figure carrying a real denominator.** Digital Education Council **LATAM survey 2026**: **>30 000 responses across 29 institutions** — **92%** of students and **79%** of faculty actively engaging with AI. UNESCO IESALC, separately: **200 higher-education institutions across 19 countries** — teaching and learning **73.5%**, research **57.0%**, administration **34.1%**, community engagement **20.0%**.

- 🔴 **The gap is the widest measured anywhere: ~62 points.** **92%** usage against **~30%** of LATAM universities having published AI policies (Tecnológico de Monterrey, in the same survey).
- 🟡 **Policy is heterogeneous and mostly non-binding.** **Chile** is furthest along — National AI Policy since 2021, a law under discussion. In **Mexico**, SEP, ANUIES and Observatorio IA have produced **recommendations with no binding force**. 🔵 **CEPAL/IDB's ILIA 2025 index covers readiness, adoption and governance across 19 countries** and is the instrument to cite in a regional bid.
- 🟢 **Administration at 34.1% against teaching at 73.5% is the clearest whitespace on this shelf**: the back office is barely touched while the classroom is saturated. **Enrolment, retention prediction, financial aid and SIS workflow** — and `rosariosis`/`openSIS-Classic` are the open SIS platforms, both GPL-2.0, both with the boundary pattern already written in `verticals/solutions.md`.
- 🟢 **Real local supply, placed on the commit channel, not inferred:** `SidneyBissoli/uis-mcp-server` (**MIT**, 1 088 B — UNESCO education statistics over MCP) and `Cvmcosta/ltijs` (Brazil, `-0300` on 513 of 575 commits). 🔵 **Two unrelated maintainers, two instruments, one region — a measurement with a denominator, not an anecdote.**
- 🟡 **`digitaleducationcouncil.com` could not be reached from this environment** (`ENOTFOUND`). The figures above are **attributed to the survey, not asserted by this base**, and the primary PDF is the first thing a future pass should fetch from a network that can reach it.
- 🟢 **Added p343 — THE FINDING FOR THIS REGION, and it was on these shelves the whole time: the Brazilian curriculum-standards layer is available, permissively licensed, and already hosted.** `bncc-dev/bncc-pacotes` + `bncc-dev/bncc-dados` + `bncc-dev/bncc-benchmark` — **MIT code, CC BY 4.0 data** — carry **1 721 learning objectives of Brazilian basic education**, which the README states are *"verificadas contra os documentos oficiais do MEC/CNE"*. They ship as `@bncc/dados` (npm **v0.7.0**, typed TS API), `bncc` (PyPI **v0.5.0**), `@bncc/mcp` (npm **v0.6.0**) **and a hosted MCP server at `mcp.bncc.dev` — streamable HTTP, NO API KEY, seven tools** (`bncc_lookup`, `bncc_buscar`, `bncc_listar`, `bncc_decodificar`, `bncc_estatisticas`, `bncc_estrutura`, `bncc_progressao_ei`). Placed **LATAM — Brazil** on both channels: `-0300` on **225 of 225** commits across the three repositories, with `profy.com.br` on **211** of them. 🔵 **A Brazilian engagement can call a national curriculum from an agent on day one and spend its budget on the platform half.**
- 🔴 **Added p343 — and the reason six passes missed it is the CHANNEL, not the region: nine stars.** Every discovery leg this base runs is star- or trending-ranked, and a nine-star repository with a production MCP server over a national curriculum is invisible to all of them. 🟢 **`ACTION ZZ-d`: query the package registries and the framework NAMES — `BNCC`, `SEP`, `Aprende`, `MEC` — not the star count.**
- 🟢 **Added p343 — the complementary gap, enumerated from an asset's own source list rather than from a silence.** `withmarbleapp/os-taxonomy` (6.6k ★, the most-cited education repository of this month) carries **3 881 standards from eight frameworks and not one of them is Latin American** — UK National Curriculum, Australian Curriculum v9, Common Core, NGSS, NCSS, IB. 🔵 **So the dominant open curriculum graph has no LATAM spine, and the LATAM spine exists separately under a CLEANER grant than that graph's own US tiers** (CC BY 4.0, against a purpose-limited CCSS licence and a codes-only NGSS tier). **That composition is this region's clearest build, and `compose/patterns.md` R-343.1 is it.**
- 🟡 **Added p343 — Mexico's first named education-touching instrument: the Chapultepec Principles**, a ten-point ethical framework for the use and development of AI presented by **SECIHTI and the ATDT in January 2026**. Non-binding. At least **six** legislative initiatives 2022–2025, **none approved**; an early-2026 Senate bill proposes a risk-classification body. 🔵 **Mexico is a guidelines market, not a compliance market — price it accordingly.** Brazil's **PL 2338/2023** remains Senate-passed (2024) and **awaiting the Chamber of Deputies**.
- 🔴 **Added at p341, and it revises how this subsection's "gap" should be read:** the regional **platform** supply was never absent, it was **unreadable** — `portabilis/i-diario` sat on these shelves for 22 passes as *"licence not shown"* because both shared family classifiers are English-only, and its Portuguese payload is **`AGPL-3.0`** (`intel/trends.md` **T31**). **Read the payload on a LATAM engagement; do not trust the sidebar or a scanner.**

- 🟢 **Added p344 — the region finally has a hard denominator, and the gap IS the engagement.** **UNESCO IESALC with UNU-IAS: 200 institutions across 19 countries — 87% use AI somewhere in operations, and only 26% have a formal AI strategy.** 🔴 **A 61-point gap.** Adoption is **faculty- and student-driven rather than institutional**, so the buyer is a rector or provost who has **already lost control of the surface and needs it mapped**. 🟡 **Private institutions have materially more planning and governance capacity than public ones** — the realistic first cohort.
- 🟢 **Added p344 — the convening structure exists and should be aligned to rather than competed with.** **UNESCO's Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean launched 2026-04-14**, with **CAF, CENIA (Chile), ECLAC, Fundación Santillana, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo and Fundación Ceibal (Uruguay)**, plus a Mexico pilot building centres of excellence with **CONALEP and DGETI**.
- 🟡 **Added p344 — regulation here is general-purpose, NOT education-specific** (Uruguay's Council of Europe convention signature, Peru's phased framework, Colombia's CONPES 4144, Mexico's Senate bill, Brazil and Chile in motion). 🟢 **So a LATAM engagement is sold on the adoption gap and institutional risk, never on a compliance deadline. That is the inverse of the EMEA and APAC pitch and must not be copied from them.**
- 🔴 **Added p344 — the supply count, stated as a count for the second consecutive pass: ONE instrument-placed LATAM row** (`Cvmcosta/ltijs`, Brazil). 🟢 **Plus the largest cross-region bench measured on these shelves — 216 commits at `-0300` by a single author inside `formalms`** — 🟡 **whose country is NOT resolvable from the available evidence: every sampled month falls inside the southern DST window, so Chile and Brazil are indistinguishable (`ACTION ZZ-d`). The band is LATAM; the country may not be written, and this creates no LATAM row.**

- 🟢 **Added p346 — the region's governance gap now has a measured width, from a first-party institutional source rather than a vendor roundup.** A **UNESCO IESALC** study of **200 institutions across 19 countries** finds **87% using AI in at least one area** and **74% in teaching and learning**, while **only 26% have a formal AI strategy** *(search channel; UNESCO IESALC and UNU-IAS, launched during Digital Learning Week 2026 — sources disagree on whether that is 2026-09-09, and the date is not asserted here)*. 🔵 **That is a ~61-point gap between use and governance, and it is the single most sellable number on this shelf for the region** — adoption is already driven bottom-up by individual academics and students, which means the buyer's problem is not persuasion but control. 🟢 **The engagement writes itself:** institutional AI strategy, acceptable-use policy and an audit trail for use that is already happening, with no platform replacement required.

- 🟡 **Added p346 — the policy channel is retrievable country by country, and no regional framework exists for education specifically.** **Uruguay** became the first LATAM country to sign the **Council of Europe Framework Convention on AI and Human Rights** (2025); **Peru** runs regulatory **sandboxes** with a phased schedule favouring smaller firms; **Mexico's** Congress is converging on an unacceptable/high/lower-risk taxonomy from a 2024 federal bill; **Colombia** adopted its national AI policy via **CONPES 4144** (February 2025). 🟢 **UNESCO's Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean launched 2026-04-14**, with a **Mexico pilot with CONALEP and DGETI** — a named, fundable counterpart for a ministry-facing engagement. 🔵 **Read commercially: there is no single rulebook to certify against, so sell per-country mapping and institutional policy, exactly as in North America, and use the UNESCO observatory as the credibility anchor.**

- 🔴 **Added p346 — the supply channel returned ONE new LATAM address this pass, and its bench ends by construction; stated rather than counted as coverage.** [`unb-mds/2025-2-InfoSchool`](https://github.com/unb-mds/2025-2-InfoSchool) is **MIT (1 059 B)** and places cleanly to **LATAM — Brazil** on `-0300` across all **415** commits (non-shifting, which is correct since Brazil ended DST in 2019) — the org is **Universidade de Brasília's** software-engineering methods course. 🔴 **Its window is 2025-08-22 → 2025-12-02: `2025-2` is an academic semester, and the bench completed rather than died** (`ACTION ZZ-p`). 🟢 **This base's instrument-placed LATAM rows therefore still stand at the three p345 promoted**, led by `Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil` (MIT, 1 817 ★, pushed daily). 🔵 **And one trap was re-confirmed here:** `eduagarcia/lm-evaluation-harness-pt` carries Portuguese in its slug, 3 279 commits and **not one `-0300` in its top three offsets** — it is EleutherAI's log, the second instance of `ACTION ZZ-h` in two passes. **Do not staff a LATAM engagement off a Portuguese-language repository name.**

- 🟢 **Added p347 — the instrument-placed LATAM count reaches FOUR, and the newest row is a university's own systems.** [`CaioCastro1/usp-mcp`](https://github.com/CaioCastro1/usp-mcp) — **MIT, 18 ★, 378 commits, 3 authors, 2026-08-31 → 2026-10-19**, `-0300` on all 378 — gives an assistant Portuguese-language access to **Universidade de São Paulo**'s refectory menus, e-Disciplinas deadlines and JupiterWeb catalogue. 🟡 **Self-declared unofficial**, so the MIT grant covers the client and USP's data is not licensed by it — the third instance in three passes of **MIT code over an unlicensed public-institution data source**. 🔵 A second USP server exists (`iDavi/usp-mcp`, 5 ★): the demand is a cluster, not a one-off.
- 🟢 **Added p347 — the regional institution is now education-specific, and there is a denominator to quote.** UNESCO's **Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean launched 2026-04-14 in Santiago**, with CAF, CENIA (Chile), CETIC.br (Brazil), ECLAC, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo and Fundación Ceibal named as partners. UNESCO IESALC's survey — **200 institutions, 19 countries, 87 % using AI in at least one area, 26 % with a formal AI strategy** — makes the **61-point governance gap** the most sellable figure on this file. 🟢 **It is a policy-and-inventory engagement, not a build**, and it is cheap to staff in a region where under a third of pilots reach a P&L.
- 🟡 **Added p347 — Mexico is placed but not promoted, and the distinction is the honest one.** `agn3si/ResultadosUNAM---data` is MIT with `astro.unam.mx` in its domain channel — a genuine **UNAM** placement — on a **13-commit** repository, so it is recorded in [`p347-institutional-orphan-probe`](../evidence/p347-institutional-orphan-probe.2026-10-20-p347.tsv) and shelved nowhere. 🔵 Country status unchanged: **Uruguay** first in the region to sign the Council of Europe AI Framework Convention, **Colombia** on CONPES 4144, **Peru** with regulatory sandboxes, **Mexico**'s federal bill still a bill.

- 🔴 **Added p348 — a REVERSAL this base should act on: stop waiting for a LATAM platform tier.** The regional token sweep over the whole 528-row permissive inventory returned **14 candidate addresses**. Read at the pin: **two** are LATAM-authored code — [`latam-gpt/anonymization-filter`](https://github.com/latam-gpt/anonymization-filter) (**MIT**, `−0400` on all 6 commits, CENIA/Chile) and [`Jeanikt/tutor-ai-agent`](https://github.com/Jeanikt/tutor-ai-agent) (**MIT**, `−0300` on all 3 commits, Brazil, a LiveKit template derivative) — one is a **Polish vendor**, one is an **EleutherAI fork** published under a LATAM org, and the rest are UK or US. 🟢 **Six passes of expecting regional supply to appear is enough: the engagement here is adoption-and-governance built on GLOBAL supply, and that is a strength to state rather than a gap to apologise for.**
- 🟢 **Added p348 — the demand numbers are the strongest of any region and they are the sales argument.** UNESCO: **nine in ten** 15-year-olds across the **13** regional PISA 2025 countries have already used AI chatbots for schoolwork. TALIS 2024: teacher AI use at **56 % in Brazil** and **55 % in Chile** against an **OECD average of 36 %**. 🔵 **Teachers here are ahead of the OECD by twenty points and institutions are behind their own students** — which is a governance engagement with a ready buyer, not a technology sale.
- 🟡 **Added p348 — Brazil is the one regional instrument that would change what you BUILD.** **PL 2338/2023** is still in process and its high-risk category **explicitly includes education**, which would bring mandatory impact assessments to schools and universities. Chile's bill is pending and principles-based; Mexico's 2026 amendments cover deepfakes, performers' rights and enforcement — **not education** — and sources disagree whether they amount to a general AI law. Tec de Monterrey is redesigning **44+** degree programmes to integrate AI. 🔵 **Design for PL 2338 now in a Brazilian engagement: an impact assessment is cheap at architecture time and expensive at audit time.**
- 🟢 **Added p348 — the one LATAM component worth composing with, and it fits the governance engagement exactly.** `latam-gpt/anonymization-filter` is a **PII-stripping filter** built to assemble LatamGPT's corpus. **Six commits, one author, dormant 17 months** — so read it as a pattern rather than a dependency. 🔵 Student-data protection is the binding duty in three of four regions, and a regional artefact that does exactly that is the right thing to show a ministry, with its size stated.

### Global


- 🟢 **Added p351 — PIN CURRICULUM DATA BY DIGEST, and put it in the contract.** `withmarbleapp/os-taxonomy` regenerated itself **2026-10-20** under an **unchanged `taxonomyVersion: v1`**: topics **1 591 → 1 616**, prerequisite edges **3 225 → 3 317**, topic↔standard links **2 189 → 3 214 (+47 %)**. 🔴 **A tutor pinned at "v1" silently re-orders its prerequisites when the upstream regenerates, and nothing in its dependency declaration changes.** 🟢 Both data rows ship **`generatedAt` plus a SHA-256 per file**, and Marble binds its own export to `topicFileSha256` — so **pin manifest digest + `generatedAt`, never the version string** (recipe `P40`, trend `T49`). 🔴 **And a permissive licence on GENERATED data is not a warranty of fact** (`T51`): `aclascollege/polymath` is cleanly ODbL + CC-BY-SA while its own `PROVENANCE.md` declares its course, degree, career and **salary-band** content *"synthesized"*. **Prefer a row that ships a `PROVENANCE.md` over a better-starred one that does not.**
- 🟢 **Added p345 — two cross-cutting facts a studio can use in any region.** 🔴 **Market sizing from this channel is unusable:** two sources attribute incompatible 2026 figures to the *same* analyst (≈ **USD 23 bn at ~42% CAGR** and **USD 12.3 bn**), so it is published as a conflict and never as a number; the adoption percentages in the same channel come from vendor pages with self-reported surveys. 🟢 **What IS reusable is the promotion discipline:** of 12 live rows a single topic query returned, **9 had sat on a trending shelf without ever being adjudicated** — one at **4 696 ★** — and this base held **528** permissively-licensed addresses it had measured and never offered. 🔵 **For a client running their own radar the lesson transfers directly: measure delivery, not intake, or you will report saturation while sitting on an unread backlog** (`intel/trends.md` **`T41`**). 🟡 **And two placement traps that cost nothing to avoid:** a repository or organisation NAME in a regional language is not a region signal (`ACTION ZZ-g`), and a FORK's commit log measures its upstream's bench (`ACTION ZZ-h`).

🟢 **What holds in every region, and is therefore the reusable part of a bid.**

- 🔴 **Usage leads permission everywhere it was measured.** 66/10 in North America, 92/30 in LATAM. The scarce good is **not capability**; it is **an auditable boundary**. That is one offer, sellable in four regions, and it is what `compose/patterns.md` R-338.1 builds.
- 🔴 **Licence risk sits in two places a standard review misses**, and both are now measured on these shelves: **CONTENT** (curriculum graphs and standards frameworks under ODbL / CC BY-SA / OGL / purpose-limited public licences — p337's T-337.1) and **PATH DEPTH** (a grant at `docs/License.txt` or `html/license.txt` that a root-only SPDX scan reports as *no licence*). 🟢 **Enumerate the tree; never trust a badge.**
- 🟢 **The permissive platform tier is exactly two deep, re-verified this pass:** `OpenOLAT` (Apache-2.0) and `JupyterHub` (BSD). **Everything else in the LMS/SIS tier is copyleft**, and the same architectural boundary answers all seven of them: unmodified core, your logic in a separate service, integrate over LTI or web services.
- 🟡 **Do not quote a market size for education AI.** Three publishers, three irreconcilable figures, three passes running (`ACTION ZQ`). **Quote the surveys with denominators instead** — they are what a client can check.
- 🔴 **Added p343 — the content-licence tier list is the reusable asset, and it is regionally INVERTED.** Read first-hand from `os-taxonomy`'s `PROVENANCE.md` (8 685 B): a curriculum-standards payload is **not** governed by its repository's headline grant. **OGL v3.0** (UK — commercial, adaptable, **no share-alike**) and **CC BY 4.0** (Australia, and Brazil via BNCC) are workable; the **CCSS Public License** is **purpose-limited** and, in that file's own words, *"does not clearly grant derivative-work or sublicensing rights"*; **NGSS** ships **codes only**, because commercial reuse of the text needs **WestEd approval** plus a trademark disclaimer; **NCSS** and **IB** are permission-based. 🟢 **Coverage and usability are different maps: the EMEA and APAC tiers of the dominant graph are its cleanest, and the North American tiers are its worst.** 🔵 **Topic→standard LINKS survive at every tier — it is the standard's PROSE that does not.** So plan a US K-12 product on codes, or licence the text directly.
- 🔴 **Added p343 — a star count and a commit count are not comparable units across repositories, and the test is one command.** `zijinz456/OpenTutor` has **52 of its 194 commits authored by `noreply@anthropic.com`, every one a `chore:`** — weekly build-in-public content, repo health reports, community post drafts, running since March. `ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp` has **30 of 467 from the same address, every one a `fix:` or `test:`** — migrations, store exceptions, a named stabilization audit. 🟢 **Same channel, opposite meaning: the axis is engineering vs promotion, and only the subject lines carry it** (`ACTION ZZ-c`). **Run `git log --format='%ae%x09%s'` before quoting any row's bench to a client.**

- 🟢 **Added p344 — the structural fact to lead any platform conversation with, now stable across passes: the education PLATFORM tier is copyleft, and the permissive tier is where the AI layer goes.** Eleven platforms measured this pass; **the intersection of *permissive* and *education domain model* is empty**, excepting the Apereo/`ECL-2.0` family. 🟢 **Prescription, licence-robust across Moodle (GPL-3.0), Open edX (Apache-2.0), OpenEduCat (LGPL-3.0) and Forma LMS (GPL-2.0) alike: agents sit OUTSIDE the platform and speak to it over its API; they never ship as patched addons.**
- 🔴 **Added p344 — grant ABSENCE, not copyleft, is the recurring blocker in the connector tier.** Of three MCP connectors re-probed, **two declare MIT in a manifest with no licence text anywhere in the tree**, and **one has neither** (all rights reserved). 🟢 **Two are a one-email fix; the third is a blocker. Check the payload, never the badge.**
- 🔴 **Added p344 — a new global risk class, measured three passes running: agent-authored repositories.** A repository can now be authored, promoted and defaulted-to by an agent while **star count, commit count and timezone offset all stay silent** (`ACTION ZZ-c`, `ZZ-f`). 🟢 **Cheap test: `git log --format='%ae%x09%s' | grep -i anthropic`.** 🔴 **At a 100% agent-authored share the region channel is VOID, not merely weak** — `davipac/classroom-mcp` is the specimen, and its `+0000` offset is a datacenter's, not a geography.
- 🔴 **Added p344 — two addresses carried on these shelves do not exist**, and both resolved one slug away with a default branch no ladder assumes (`openeducat/openeducat` → `openeducat/openeducat_erp@19.0`; `forma-lms/formalms` → `formalms/formalms`, grant at `html/license.txt`). 🟢 **Before relying on any row, read its default ref from `ls-remote --symref` and enumerate the tree (`ACTION ZZ-e`).**
- 🟡 **Added p344 — market sizing remains a RANGE, not a number** (unchanged from p339). The figures this pass's leg returned — a **$12.3B** 2026 size and an **83%** survey number — arrived **without verifiable sourcing from a vendor page and are NOT adopted.**

- 🟢 **Added p346 — verifiable learner credentials moved from a standards conversation into Linux Foundation governance, and the code is MIT.** [`openwallet-foundation-labs/learner-credential-wallet`](https://github.com/openwallet-foundation-labs/learner-credential-wallet) (**MIT, 1 091 B, 1 309 commits, iOS + Android**) is the same repository this base could have shelved under `digitalcredentials/learner-credential-wallet` — **identical HEAD `1c46a82`** — and the canonical address is now an **OpenWallet Foundation** lab. 🔵 **The migration is the finding:** a licence cell is identical on both addresses and shows none of it, so **governance has to be read separately from the grant**. 🟢 **Against 1EdTech's read that digital credentials become a core mechanism for skills-based learning and hiring, this is the reference implementation with a foundation behind it** — the lowest-risk credential component to put in front of a ministry or a university system.

- 🟡 **Added p346 — the offline-first authoring half of Kolibri is permissive and live, and it is the row to reach for wherever connectivity is the constraint.** [`learningequality/studio`](https://github.com/learningequality/studio) — **MIT, 212 ★, 15 505 commits, pushed today**, default ref **`unstable`** — is the content curation and authoring system behind Kolibri, which this base already shelves. 🔵 **Per `ACTION ZZ-b` its placed cell is authorship (North America — United States, PST/PDT confirmed) while its SUBJECT is global**: Kolibri's deployment base is low-connectivity schooling, which is where the MEA and rural-LATAM engagements actually land. 🟢 **Pairing it with the curriculum-ontology rows gives a studio an authoring pipeline and a standards spine that both work without a network.**

- 🔴 **Added p346 — a structural warning for every market cell on this shelf, learned from two licence payloads rather than from a market report.** Education AI in both of this pass's new curriculum rows is built on **public-sector curriculum data whose code grant is permissive and whose DATA rights are explicitly withheld** — Korea's official curriculum documents in `DECK6/korean-elementary-learning-map`, Brazil's public-institution marks and third-party API data in `Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil`. 🟢 **So the recurring commercial risk in this sector is not the licence, it is the corpus**: a product can be fully MIT and still be unshippable because the curriculum text, assessment items or institutional marks it renders were never granted. 🔵 **Put a data-rights review next to the licence review in every education engagement** — and expect the answer to be a per-source register, as both rows provide.

- 🟢 **Added p347 — the supply-side mechanism is now proven three passes running, and it is not search.** Every agent and platform row promoted at p345, p346 and p347 came out of this base's own **528-address permissive-orphan inventory** read with an institutional filter; the four prescribed discovery legs returned their **thirteenth** consecutive zero. 🟡 **The filter's false-positive rate is measured, not estimated: 6 of the 100 unread education-token orphans are machine-UNLEARNING repositories, matched because `learn` is a substring of `unlearning`** — so quote yield against 94, not 100.
- 🔴 **Added p347 — market sizing stays unquotable, and this pass can say what to use instead.** The same legs returned, for the same year: **$6.4 B**, **$7.52 B → $10.6 B**, **$10.6 B → $42.48 B by 2030**, **$136.79 B by 2035**, and **$2.85 B for APAC alone**. 🟢 **A ten-fold disagreement between vendor estimates is an absence of a measurement.** What is defensible is a denominator with a method (200 institutions / 19 countries / 87 % / 26 %) and a **commit channel**: 19 717 commits and 122 authors on one national service says more about what education software costs than any of those five figures.

- 🔴 **Added p348 — the three loudest numbers in this industry are the three least usable, and this pass measured all three spreads.** **Market size**: AI-in-education 2026 at **USD 9.58B / 10.6B / 11.4B** from three firms with 2030–2035 endpoints of **42.48B** and **136.79B**, while the narrower AI-tutors segment runs **2.7B → 17.7B (2033)** from one firm and **6.6B → 80.4B (2034)** from another — an order of magnitude apart on the same object. **Stars**: 4 ★ on a live national service with 5 266 commits, 1 ★ on a suite pushed today, 21 ★ on a seven-year university platform, 2 300 ★ on the row with fewer commits than any of them. **"Authors"**: `%an` 14 / `%ae` 18 / `%an|%ae` 20 on one repository at one pin, with a spread of 1–9 non-zero on 8 of 8 rows measured. 🔵 **One failure, three times: a single scalar standing in for a definition nobody wrote down.**
- 🟢 **Added p348 — what to quote instead, and it is six fields because what a client inherits does not compress to one.** *grant at the pin · commits · author NAMES · first commit · last push · timezone distribution.* Every one is first-hand from a blobless clone plus `raw.githubusercontent.com`, both of which answer from this environment, and none of them depends on `api.github.com` (403 here).
- 🔴 **Added p348 — a filter lesson that applies to every industry KB, not only this one.** Three of four regions' binding instruments turn on student-data protection, demonstrated non-discrimination, or a human decision between model and consequence. **None of the components that answer those duties carries an education token** — group-fairness libraries, privacy and synthetic-data tooling, audit logging. 🟡 **An industry KB that selects on industry vocabulary is structurally blind to the HORIZONTAL components that industry's regulation demands**, which is why a 2 300-star MIT library effectively required by EMEA's Annex III duty took 348 passes to reach these shelves.

## Pass 337 · 2026-10-20 — eighth consecutive pass with no new supply from the prescribed legs, and the first one where the reason is measured as SATURATION

🔴 **The four prescribed global legs were run verbatim and returned 0 new promotable repository slugs. Eighth consecutive measurement.** What is new this pass is the *reason*, which is not the same as the previous seven:

| leg, run verbatim 2026-10-20 | what came back |
|---|---|
| `top open source AI agents education 2026 github MIT` | 🔴 General agent round-ups with **no education ranking at all** — OpenHands, OpenClaw, CrewAI, LangGraph. One source's own caveat: AgentGPT is **GPL-3.0 and archived 2026-01-28**. |
| `github trending education AI 2026` | 🔴 Infrastructure and dev-tool repos. The single education-relevant hit, `withmarbleapp/os-taxonomy`, **this base added at p326.** |
| `open source platform education ERP CRM MIT Apache` | 🟡 LMS/SIS vendor and review pages. **Confirmed: there is no mainstream MIT-licensed LMS or SIS** — Open edX is AGPL, Moodle/Chamilo/ILIAS GPL, Canvas CE AGPL, RosarioSIS GPL-2.0. 🟢 **`OpenOLAT` is the Apache-2.0 exception** and is re-verified first-hand this pass at 10 982 B. |
| `AI education industry trends 2026` | 🔴 Vendor trend blogs with **conflicting market sizes** ($6B 2024→$32B 2030 vs $12.3B by 2026). Not usable as a figure; recorded as a conflict. |

🟢 **The saturation measurement, which is the new fact.** The one leg returning education-specific repositories gave 8 candidate slugs; **6 were already `CARRIED`** and the 2 new ones are below bar (10 commits / 2 commits). 🔵 **6-of-8-already-held is a different signal from 0-of-8-found, and it is the healthier one:** it says the channel's education-agent supply has been absorbed, not that the channel is dry.

🔴 **A cross-cutting licence conflict worth carrying, from the search layer itself:** two separate comparison pages list **Forma LMS** as Apache-2.0 and as GPL-v2 respectively. 🟡 **Unresolved — not probed this pass, and not published as either.** Registered as the next licence question for this tier.
